<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003253 -->
# Actual off-line continuation and a fully initialized late return

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max4; analytical invocation71.
Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED. No new fact is asserted or submitted.
Baseline ended70; authorized ended ceiling71 / attempted ceiling72;
controller cap1; consult OFF. All earlier budgets remain closed.

Two concrete static laws are derived on the FULL, SAME enclosing-m
tau=1/4 hierarchy. The first preserves every four-query entry jet,
takes the actual off-line gradient, changes to the retained y,z face,
and has an explicit positive terminal value through every remaining
physical step. Nevertheless its exact original benchmark ratio is
strictly less than9/16 at every enclosing m>=7. The second pays the
entire initialized prefix to the actual last-left-leaf/root/first-right-
leaf return. Its named necessary product guard passes, all actual face
guards also pass, but the NEXT original leaf enters the minimum cone.
The complete later right child is executed with zero gradients, so its
terminal is0. Every completion preserving this entire queried prefix
also has terminal0 and true radius1.

These are scoped failures of the specified laws, not upper bounds on
unrestricted W_n(H_m). The original arbitrary-word theorem remains OPEN.
No third mutation, automatic renewal, formal gate, outward publication,
finalization, revocation, shutdown or original-Goal completion is inferred.

## 1. Steering, accepted inputs, notation and the original benchmark

TASK.md and dispatch master_guidance50cac122a7a68f0f were read in full.
Later master_guidance87f657463efe08da was read in full and explicitly
leaves max4r71 unchanged, with zero additional gate or computation.
Full prior findings d1d4637e7e54abd4 and02fd1fa1baa2b1f7 were recalled
as UNVERIFIED awareness. Their geometry is re-derived jointly below;
none is a correctness premise.

Accepted fact_id c3bfc54321ddec65 was read in FULL, including its proof,
its A_mc>=4B_mc+4, B_mc>=2, M_mc>=2 hypotheses, its additional return
condition M_mc*epsilon>=2B_mc^2, and its two SPECIAL growing/padded word
families. Neither of the laws below is silently placed inside that
parameter contract. That fact does not cover arbitrary small first
ratios, arbitrary words, or this hierarchy's new continuation.

The actual correctness premises used below are:

* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290, full constructive maximal-prefix interpolation
  proof and original signed residual necessity. For queried jets plus
  optimum0, u_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 give the explicit
  completion [||v||^2/2+max_i(u_i.v-delta_i)]^*. It preserves EVERY
  promised jet and is maximal for THAT complete prefix. A prior anchor
  cannot be deleted. The signed original residual is
  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i.(x_j-x_i)+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.
* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d, exact original all-split recurrences and the
  legal single-cut/global-Huber channel K_n. In particular the same-word
  Huber payoff is1/(1+2 sum h), attained with true initial radius1.
* fact_id04219554e41d9750, its exact definitions and the elementary
  growth argument in proof Sections3 and9, specifically
  B_n<=4C_n<=4(n+1)^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt2). The applicable passages
  were read. This is its UNCONDITIONAL scalar bound, not its conditional
  seed-lift assertion. No certificate seed is assumed.

For nonnegative x,y define

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

The exact ORIGINAL benchmark is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every split is an original integer split. For a nonnegative physical
word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum over all finite dimensions,
convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient, minimizers
x_star and x_0!=x_star of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),
    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t),     L>0.

All witnesses below use the COMPLETE minimum set and its TRUE closest
initial distance. Norms and inner products are Euclidean. The standard
basis of R^3 is e1,e2,e3. A star sample means x_star=g_star=f_star=0.
conv denotes convex hull; partial denotes convex subdifferential.
All max-affine weights in this report are1. Each offset is printed.
For a real z, z_-=min(z,0). A squared slope norm called Q or R is
explicitly local to its policy, not the original objective R_n=W_n/2.
The accompanying glossary gives distinct shared names for these aliases.

## 2. The unchanged complete physical hierarchy

Fix ANY integer m>=7 and set

    u=1/(4m), lambda=1+sqrt2,
    beta=1-u, c=lambda*(1+u), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l),   0<=l<m,
    b_l=1+a*c^l, h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S_l=sum H_l.

Every child uses this SAME enclosing m and tau=1/4. There is no prefix
reset, child restart, step permutation, omitted update, or padding.
Every original h_t>1 is retained. Its exact index is

    h_t=b_(nu_2(t+1)),     0<=t<n,

where nu_2(j) is the largest nonnegative integer exponent with2^nu_2(j)
dividing the positive integer j. Induct on H_(l+1): the center has
t+1=2^l, and right-child indices use
nu_2(2^l+j)=nu_2(j) for0<j<2^l.

Define v_h=a/(c-2). The full physical mass is exactly

    1+S_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l.                         (1)

The induction is1+S_(l+1)=2(1+S_l)+a*c^l. Here beta>=27/28,
2<c<3, a<1/2 and0<v_h<1: c-2>lambda-2=1/lambda>a.
Also a=beta/c>9/28, so v_h>a>9/28. All h_t>1 give S_m>n.

For the first law put

    M=2+a, s=M-1=1+a, E=beta*c, Delta=E-M.

The cancellation is exact:

    Delta=2u/[lambda*(1+u)]-lambda*u^2,
    u/2<Delta<2u<=1/14.                              (2)

Indeed lambda<5/2, u<=1/28 imply
2/[lambda*(1+u)]>112/145>3/4 and lambda*u<5/56<1/10;
thus Delta/u>13/20>1/2. Dropping the negative term and lambda>1
gives the upper bound. In particular2<M<5/2, 1<s<3/2 and
E=M+Delta. Write Bgeo=M+E=2M+Delta<6; Bgeo is NOT B_n.

The accepted benchmark bound at the ACTUAL horizon gives

    B_n<=4(n+1)^rho=4lambda^m.                       (3)

For clarity its direct all-split step is
G(x,4y)<=4Omega(x,y); induction yields B_n<=4C_n, and the
accepted elementary Omega power bound yields C_n<=(n+1)^rho.
Balanced splitting is not assumed for B_n.

Combining(1) with c>lambda and lambda^m>2^m>=128 gives

    1+2S_m>(9/14)lambda^m-1>(1/2)lambda^m,
    B_n/(1+2S_m)<8.                                 (4)

The second strict mass inequality follows from lambda^m/7>1.
No asymptotic profile constant or executed recurrence is needed.

## 3. Policy1: static preserving curvature with an actual off-line step

Every parameter in this policy is specified from the known full word
BEFORE running GD. Define

    d=a/[2(1+beta)], p=e1,
    q=-d*e1+sqrt(a*d-d^2)*e2, Q=||q||^2=a*d,
    k=beta/(2M), r=-k*q+k*sqrt(Q)*e3,
    R=||r||^2=2k^2 Q,
    y=-a*e1-q,
    z=y-beta*q-M*r=y-(beta/2)*(q+sqrt(Q)*e3),
    tR=z+s*r.

The radicand is positive since0<d<a. Direct products give

    y.q=y.r=z.r=0, z.q=-beta*Q/2,
    q.r=-kQ, tR.r=sR,
    y_1=-a+d<0,
    z_1=-a+(1+beta/2)d<0,
    (tR)_1=-a+(1+beta-k)d<-a/2.

The auxiliary homogeneous reference is the SINGLE static function

    Dentry=conv{0,p,q,r},
    Fentry(x)=max_(g in Dentry){x.g-||g||^2/2},
    Jentry(w)=max(0,p.w,q.w,r.w).

Its gradient is Euclidean projection onto Dentry. A pair x=w+g
has that gradient iff g belongs to Dentry and
w.b<=w.g for every b in Dentry. Then
Fentry(x)=Jentry(w)+||g||^2/2. The following exhaustive guards
derive its four ORIGINAL entry jets, without adopting the prior draft:

| query | actual x | gradient | proximal w | value | every support guard |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|0|e1|p|0|1/2|all four planes have height0; x=p is in Dentry|
|1|-a e1=y+q|q|y|Q/2|zero,q,r tie at0; p.y<0|
|2|y-beta q=tR+r|r|tR|sR+R/2|r height sR>0; q height=(-beta+k)Q<0; p height<0; zero0|
|3|z+r|r|z|R/2|zero,r tie0; q.z<0; p.z<0|

The actual first steps are(b_0,b_1,b_0,b_2), so these reach
x_4=z-E r. Projection uniqueness resolves all support ties.
For any two such reference jets the FULL ordered residual cancels to

    Q_ij=(x_j-g_j).(g_i-g_j)<=0.                     (5)

This includes the zero optimum and every remote orientation: it is the
normal condition at j with comparison slope g_i. Thus the conjugate
construction of accepted1252 applies to the complete entry.

The entire old four-query anchor list is precisely

    (anchor,offset)=(0,0),(y,0),(z,0),(tR,sR).        (6)

The zero optimum and the p query duplicate the0 term; both physical
samples remain. The FIRST r query is tR with POSITIVE offset sR.
The q-stage has no unselected step, so its first and last anchors
coincide at y. These are not deleted or replaced by terminal guesses.

Freeze the following SINGLE law before any update:

    eta=8(1+S_m), T=1+eta,
    Heta(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,y.v,z.v,tR.v-sR)
                         +(eta/2)*(v_3)_-^2,
    Feta(x)=sup_v{x.v-Heta(v)}, x_0=e1.             (7)

The four max-affine offsets are0,0,0,-sR, all weights1. The last
term is an explicit convex C1 one-sided quadratic. This changes the
function selected BEFORE GD. No function is changed later.

At each promised entry gradient p,q,r,r its penalty and its derivative
are0, since the third coordinates are0,0,k*sqrt(Q),k*sqrt(Q).
The unchanged entry conjugate conditions are
x_0=p+0, x_1=q+y, x_2=r+tR, x_3=r+z. The exact four values also
survive. Consequently the actual run of Feta reaches the displayed
x_4=z-E r using every original entry step.

### 3.1 Global regularity, minimum, radius and all interpolation rows

Heta is finite continuous1-strongly convex with quadratic growth.
Every x has a unique conjugate maximizer g, characterized by
x in partial Heta(g). Adding the two strong subgradient inequalities
at x,x' yields

    (x-x').(g-g')>=||g-g'||^2.

The maximization inequalities then prove differentiability with
grad Feta=g; Cauchy--Schwarz gives a globally1-Lipschitz gradient.
Feta is convex and everywhere finite. This is the direct constructive
argument from accepted1252, with the additional explicit convex term.

At v=0 the penalty derivative is0. The active affine slopes are
EXACTLY0,y,z, since the tR term has strictly negative offset.
Therefore the COMPLETE minimum set and minimum value are

    Ceta=conv{0,y,z}, min Feta=0.                    (8)

Indeed a zero conjugate maximizer is equivalent to x in
partial Heta(0), and Heta(0)=0. Every nonzero vertex in(8) has
negative first coordinate. Origin is the unique closest minimizer to
e1, at TRUE distance1. For small positive first and third coordinates
of v, all nonzero affine terms are strictly negative and v_3>0;
Heta=||v||^2/2 on a nonempty open neighborhood. Its conjugate has
Hessian I there, so its least global smoothness is EXACTLY1.

For ANY two actual jets of this ONE function, including every future
jet, terminal jet and any minimizer jet, all original ordered rows hold.
Explicitly apply the unit-gradient descent inequality to
K(x)=Feta(x)-grad Feta(x_i).x, whose convex minimum is x_i:

    K(x_i)<=K(x_j)-||grad K(x_j)||^2/2.

Rearrangement is Q_ij<=0 in the original orientation. This proves
EVERY remote row in both orientations; no adjacency test is substituted.
Every actual Gram is a Gram of the displayed R^3 vectors, hence PSD.

### 3.2 The actual first off-line gradient and the next face

Introduce qhat=q/sqrt(Q), Cscale=k*sqrt(Q). The vectors qhat,e3
are orthonormal and orthogonal to the nonzero vector y. Write

    x=y+Cscale*(X*qhat+Y*e3),
    g=Cscale*(v_X*qhat+v_Y*e3).

In these coordinates the retained affine slopes relative to y are

    y-y=(0,0), z-y=Cscale*(-M,-M),
    tR-y=Cscale*(-(2M-1),-1),
    offset(tR)/Cscale^2=2s.                         (9)

At every candidate gradient in this plane y.g=0, so the0 affine
term is tied with the y term whenever the latter is active. The
subgradient combination may use weight0 on0 and weight1 on the
y,z,tR anchors; this pays the ACTUAL y component, not a reset.

Our specified eta satisfies

    eta>8*2^m>=1024,
    eta>2M/Delta.                                   (10)

By(2),2M/Delta<40m, while2^m>5m for all m>=7; the latter follows
from the m=7 bound and induction. Thus(10) is an analytic parameter
check, not an assumed region rule.

At the FIRST new query x_4 the coordinates are
(X_4,Y_4)=(Delta,-Bgeo). Put

    Lgap=Delta-Bgeo/T>0,
    g_4=Cscale*(Delta*qhat-(Bgeo/T)*e3).             (11)

Its third coordinate is negative. The y and0 affine values are0,
while

    z.g_4=-M*Cscale^2*Lgap<0,
    (tR.g_4-sR)-z.g_4=s*(r.g_4-R)<0,
    r.g_4=-Cscale^2*(Delta+Bgeo/T)<0.

All four anchors have been checked. The exact subgradient equation is
x_4=y+g_4+eta*(g_4)_3 e3. Uniqueness gives the actual gradient(11),
and conjugate evaluation gives

    2Feta(x_4)=Cscale^2*(Delta^2+Bgeo^2/T).           (12)

The qhat coefficient is Delta and the negative e3 coefficient is
Bgeo/T<Delta. Thus this gradient is NOT parallel to r. In particular
the disproved scalar formula from the preceding round is not used.

The NEXT ORIGINAL step is h_4=b_0=s. Its ACTUAL update gives

    X_5=-a*Delta,
    Y_5=-Bgeo*(1-s/T),
    U_5=X_5+Y_5=-2M-s*Lgap,
    D_5=X_5-Y_5=2M+(1-a)*Delta-s*Bgeo/T.             (13)

These pay the original leaf update using the actual off-line gradient.
The bounds in(2),(10) imply

    M<D_5<Bgeo<6,
    0<s*Lgap<s*Delta<3/28.                          (14)

For the lower D_5 bound, s*Bgeo/T<1, so D_5>2M-1>M.
For the upper bound, D_5=Bgeo-a*Delta-s*Bgeo/T<Bgeo.
Notice U_5<-2M. Thus the new position is NOT on the old z+rho*r
line, which would require U=-2M.

### 3.3 A proved retained-face region containing EVERY remaining query

For this subsection define

    delta=2/(eta+2),
    P_t=product_(5<=j<t)(1-delta*h_j),    5<=t<=n,
    D_t=D_5*P_t, U_t=U_5,
    X_t=(U_5+D_t)/2, Y_t=(U_5-D_t)/2,
    v_t=D_t/(eta+2),
    theta_t=(-U_5-eta*v_t)/(2M).                    (15)

An empty product is1. These are PRINTED symbolic formulas, not an
executed trajectory, recurrence, or arithmetic replay.

Every factor is strictly positive, since
0<delta*h_j<=2S_m/[8(1+S_m)+2]<1/4. For any finite subset of
the suffix, the inequality product(1-z_j)>=1-sum z_j for0<=z_j<=1
follows by induction, retaining the nonnegative cross term. Hence

    3/4<P_t<=1,
    (3/4)D_5<D_t<=D_5,
    v_t>0.                                         (16)

The ACTIVE WEIGHTS are checked for ALL these actual indices:

* theta_5>0, because solving(13) gives
  theta_5=-(T*X_5+Y_5)/[M*(eta+2)], with X_5<0 and Y_5<0.
* v_t<=v_5 implies theta_t>=theta_5>0.
* eta>=8 gives eta/(eta+2)>=4/5. By(14),(16),
  eta*v_t>(3/5)M>6/5>s*Lgap=-U_5-2M. Therefore theta_t<1.

Thus0<theta_t<1 for EVERY5<=t<=n. No unconstrained future weight
or unbounded invariant is assumed.

At the proposed ACTUAL gradient

    g_t=Cscale*v_t*(qhat-e3)=-v_t*r,                (17)

the0,y,z affine values are all0, while the retained first-r anchor
has value

    tR.g_t-sR=s*(r.g_t-R)
              =-2s*Cscale^2*(v_t+1)<0.            (18)

Every old affine guard is therefore paid. The penalty derivative is
-eta*Cscale*v_t e3. The admissible combination of active slopes is
(1-theta_t)y+theta_t z, and equations(15) give the exact strong
subgradient equation

    x_t=g_t+eta*(g_t)_3 e3
                     +(1-theta_t)y+theta_t z.      (19)

Uniqueness proves(17) is the actual gradient, with no alternate-face
oracle. The active combination has y component1, as the actual x_t
requires. The original initialized update at EACH t with5<=t<n is

    X_(t+1)=X_t-h_t*v_t,
    Y_(t+1)=Y_t+h_t*v_t,
    U_(t+1)=U_5,
    D_(t+1)=(1-delta*h_t)D_t.                      (20)

Equations(19),(20), with actual x_5 from(13), prove by induction the
entire full right and left subword chronology without any child reset.
This is a NEW affine carrier with U=U_5<-2M, obtained AFTER the actual
off-line step; it is not the refuted carrier through z.

Conjugate evaluation at(17) is exact, because the max-affine value
is0 and y.g_t=0:

    2Feta(x_t)=Cscale^2*D_t^2/(eta+2).               (21)

For completeness each actual NEW conjugate jet has

    u_t=x_t-g_t
       =y+Cscale*(-M*theta_t*qhat
                         +(-eta*v_t-M*theta_t)*e3),
    delta_t=Feta(x_t)-||g_t||^2/2
           =(eta/2)*Cscale^2*v_t^2,                (22)

and the off-line jet has
u_4=y-eta*Cscale*(Bgeo/T)e3,
delta_4=(eta/2)*Cscale^2*Bgeo^2/T^2.
Every corresponding lower quadratic
||v||^2/2+u_t.v-delta_t is globally dominated by Heta via its strong
subgradient inequality at g_t. Thus ALL newly reached anchors, not
just the old four, are paid by this same function. Together with the
all-pair necessity argument this includes every remote old/new row.

### 3.4 The actual unqueried terminal and ORIGINAL B_n comparison

At the ACTUAL unqueried horizon n=2^m-1 the explicit normalized payoff is

    Veta=2Feta(x_n)
         =k^2 Q * D_5^2/[8(1+S_m)+2]
                    *product_(5<=j<n)(1-2h_j/[8(1+S_m)+2])^2
         >0.                                      (23)

Its true radius is1 by(8), and its smoothness is sharply1. Formula(23)
is evaluated on the SAME frozen function(7), with all physical steps
included. It does prevent complete absorption in this declared policy,
but a positive terminal by itself is not the original target.

Let Hbase=1/(1+2S_m), the accepted SAME-WORD global Huber member of K_n.
We have k<1/4, Q=a^2/[2(1+beta)]<1/8 and therefore k^2 Q<1/128.
By(14),D_5<6; by(16),P_n<=1. Since
(1+2S_m)/(8S_m+10)<1/4,

    0<Veta/Hbase
      <(k^2 Q)*36/4<9/128<1/14.                   (24)

Combining(24) with the EXACT ORIGINAL horizon comparison(4) gives

    0<B_n*Veta<9/16<1,     EVERY m>=7.             (25)

Thus the SPECIFIED full-run law falls below U_n^ZJ by a uniform scoped
margin of at least7/16. It is also uniformly below the same-word global
Huber witness. This is NOT a bound on W_n(H_m), which maximizes over
other functions. No claim B_n*Veta->0 is made or needed here.

## 4. Policy2: the actual initialized last-leaf/root/first-leaf return

All symbols in this section are fresh policy-local aliases, distinct
from the Section3 slopes and parameters. Keep the SAME h=H_m and m.
Define the actual selected physical indices

    j=2^(m-1)-3,
    i_left=j+1=2^(m-1)-2,
    i_root=j+2=2^(m-1)-1,
    i_right=j+3=2^(m-1).

The selected INITIAL bridge at j has level1 and excess beta; the
following three original steps have excesses a,a*c^(m-1),a.
In particular the q-r-q query selection is precisely
last-left-leaf/root/first-right-leaf, not an abstract three-step word.

Pay the FULL earlier prefix by

    Pprefix=sum_(0<=t<j)h_t=S_(m-1)-(1+beta)-(1+a),
    M0=1+Pprefix=S_(m-1)-1-beta-a,
    A=beta/M0, D=a, E=a*c^(m-1),
    d=A/[2(1+D)], k=(1+D)/(1+E),
    p=e1/M0,
    q=-d*e1+sqrt(A*d-d^2)*e2, Q=||q||^2=A*d,
    r=-k*q+sqrt((D*k-k^2)*Q)*e3,
    R=||r||^2=D*k*Q,
    y=-A*e1-q, z=y-D*q-r, w=z-E*r-q,
    xabs=w-D*q.                                    (26)

All original entries before j, including ALL their earlier large
bridges, are in Pprefix. Their physical number is j; none is ignored
or independently reinitialized. They give M0>1 since j>=61 and all
h_t>1. The first selected ratio A=beta/M0 can be very small.

The genuinely changed turning coefficient k is calibrated to the
ACTUAL root bridge, rather than the old first-occurrence ratio. The
necessary product is

    D*E=beta^2*c^(m-3)>9>1+D.                      (27)

For(27), beta>3/4, c>2 and m>=7 give
beta^2*c^(m-3)>(9/16)*2^(m-3)>=9, while1+D<3/2.
In particular0<k<D, equivalent to DE>1. Hence the radicands in(26)
are strictly positive. The slopes p,q,r are linearly independent.

Fix BEFORE GD the SINGLE homogeneous envelope

    Dlate=conv{0,p,q,r},
    Jlate(u)=max(0,p.u,q.u,r.u),
    Flate(x)=max_(g in Dlate){x.g-||g||^2/2},
    x_0=e1.                                        (28)

All four primal support-plane weights are1 and all offsets are0.
The function, including the tiny root-dependent r slope, is fixed for
the known entire word before the initialized prefix is run.

### 4.1 Every original prefix guard and the actual q-r-q return

For0<=t<=j let Cprefix_t=sum_(0<=i<t)h_i. By nonnegativity,
0<=Cprefix_t<=Pprefix=M0-1. The ACTUAL p-stage query is

    x_t=e1-Cprefix_t*p,
    u_t=x_t-p=e1*(1-(Cprefix_t+1)/M0).              (29)

Its first coordinate is nonnegative. EVERY support comparison is

    p.u_t=(u_t)_1/M0>=0,
    q.u_t=-d*(u_t)_1<=0,
    r.u_t=k*d*(u_t)_1<=p.u_t,
    zero height0<=p.u_t.

The third inequality is strictly paid by

    M0*k*d=k*beta/[2(1+D)]=beta/[2(1+E)]<1.

Thus every p-stage physical update, including earlier large bridges,
has the actual gradient p by projection uniqueness. Its value is

    Flate(x_t)=1/M0-(Cprefix_t+1/2)/M0^2.

At the original query j, x_j=p and u_j=0. Its actual step1+beta
lands at x_(j+1)=-A e1=y+q.

The literal geometry paying all subsequent support guards is

    y.q=y.r=0, y_1=-A+d<0,
    z.q=(-D+k)Q<0, z.r=0,
    z_1=-A+(1+D-k)d<-A/2,
    w.q=0, w.r=kQ(1-DE)<0,
    w_1=-A+d<0.                                    (30)

To derive w exactly, use(E+1)k=D+1 in
w=y-(D+1)q-(E+1)r. Its q component cancels and
w=y-(E+1)*sqrt((Dk-k^2)Q)*e3. This gives the w identities in(30).
The z identities use q.r=-kQ and R=DkQ; the p signs follow from
(1+D)d=A/2. Thus each stated product is a direct check of(26).

The complete realized turn is

| original query | x | gradient | proximal point | value | all support guards |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
|j|p|p|0|1/(2M0^2)|all planes0; p is a permitted projection|
|i_left=j+1|y+q=-A e1|q|y|Q/2|q,r,zero tie0; p.y<0|
|i_root=j+2|z+r=y-Dq|r|z|R/2|r,zero tie0; q.z<0; p.z<0|
|i_right=j+3|w+q=z-Er|q|w|Q/2|q,zero tie0; r.w<0; p.w<0|

All query points follow by the literal initialized updates
1+beta,1+D,1+E in physical order. The earlier prefix is(29), not
an assumed starting point near the root. Support ties again determine
unique projection gradients. This proves SUFFICIENCY for this fully
declared static law's actual q-r-q turn, beyond the old necessary test.

### 4.2 The next actual leaf and the FULL later right child

The ORIGINAL step at i_right is b_0=1+D. Its actual update is

    x_(j+4)=(w+q)-(1+D)q=xabs=w-Dq.

Every polar inequality is STRICT:

    q.xabs=-D Q<0,
    r.xabs=kQ(1+D-DE)<0 by(27),
    (xabs)_1=-A+(1+D)d=-A/2<0.                    (31)

The COMPLETE minimum set of(28) and its value are

    Clate={x:p.x<=0,q.x<=0,r.x<=0}, min Flate=0.     (32)

These inequalities suffice in the defining maximum. If any one
inner product is positive, a sufficiently small positive multiple of
that slope lies in Dlate and yields positive value, proving necessity.
Thus xabs is a minimum, and EVERY later original physical query and
update is exactly

    x_t=xabs, g_t=0, Flate(x_t)=0,
    j+4<=t<=n,
    x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t*0=xabs for j+4<=t<n.            (33)

The whole later right child is retained, with no truncation: the
large original steps after i_right act on the actual zero gradients.
Query j+4=2^(m-1)+1 is strictly preterminal since m>=7. In particular
the zero jet is a PHYSICAL queried old jet, not merely the unqueried
terminal or an imagined support point.

Since p=e1/M0, every minimum in(32) has first coordinate<=0.
Origin is the unique closest one to e1 and the TRUE radius is1.
The projection normal argument gives a finite convex C1 globally
1-smooth Flate; on the nonempty interior of Dlate its gradient is the
identity, so its least smoothness is EXACTLY1.

The complete ordered interpolation formula for ANY two actual jets,
including the optimum, every remote p-stage query, the q-r-q turn,
ALL zero repetitions and terminal, is

    Q_ij=u_j.(g_i-g_j)<=0,
    u_j=x_j-g_j,
    g_j in argmax_(b in Dlate)u_j.b.               (34)

Derive(34) by substituting Flate(x_i)=u_i.g_i+||g_i||^2/2
into the original signed residual and cancelling all u_i terms.
It is EXACTLY the normal condition at j with permitted slope g_i;
every orientation and every retained comparison is included. The
actual Gram is a Gram in R^3 and is PSD.

The ACTUAL unqueried terminal and ORIGINAL benchmark ratio are

    Vlate=2Flate(x_n)/dist(e1,Clate)^2=0,
    B_n*Vlate=0,       EVERY m>=7.                 (35)

The necessary debt D*E>1 is therefore paid and the whole static
turn is legal, yet this law yields no terminal gain after its complete
physical right child. The debt test alone does not supply a profile
payoff or avoid future absorption.

### 4.3 Entire-prefix completions cannot repair THIS observed absorption

This is a completion-class diagnostic for policy2, not a third
independent mutation or a claim about every late-checkpoint strategy.

The exact full old prefix, including all physical optimum queries,
has the following distinct conjugate affine anchors:

    (0,0), (tP,deltaP), (y,0), (z,0), (w,0), (xabs,0),

All p-stage intervening anchors and offsets are affine in the actual

For example one may fix BEFORE running the full word its explicit

    Hlate_full(v)=||v||^2/2
                   +max(0,tP.v-deltaP,y.v,z.v,w.v,xabs.v),

Accepted1252 and(34) preserve EVERY queried old point, gradient and

More generally consider ANY globally convex C1 1-smooth completion
of this ENTIRE queried prefix, preserving all values/gradients and

The true closest radius is STILL1 for EVERY such completion, not an

    Q_(0,star)=f_0+p.(x_star-e1)+||p||^2/2
             =(x_star)_1/M0<=0.

Thus every new minimum has first coordinate<=0; the retained origin
is the unique closest one to e1 at distance1. This proves terminal0

## 5. Exact scopes, counterexample tests and the remaining obligations

The following two tempting claims fail within this joint UNVERIFIED
derivation, with ALL their relevant legality assumptions retained:

1. Claim: any preserving off-line curvature policy that survives the
   full H_m with positive terminal value meets the original benchmark.

   arbitrary curvature/additional-face policies remain open.

   supplies a positive full-terminal witness. Law(28) has D*E>9,
   every earlier p step and every selected support guard paid, yet
   its NEXT original leaf reaches the minimum and its complete
   terminal is0. Every completion preserving its ENTIRE queried
   prefix also remains0. The impact excludes only this named law
   and that exact completion class, not arbitrary checkpoint choices,
   positive affine heights, multiframe turns or future face mutations.

No extra literature layer is needed to reject a new black-box regime:

The following are SEPARATE still-unproved theorem-level obligations:

* An arbitrary-word construction/decomposition: for EVERY sufficiently

* A word-independent profile bound for that construction:
  W_n(h)>=(1-epsilon_n)/B_n with epsilon_n>=0 and epsilon_n->0
  independent of the word. Exact original B_n and actual horizon n
  must be used. A selected hierarchy family or positive finite
  terminal value does not meet either obligation.

The actual eta=4M/Delta policy from round70 is not given a fictional
full-terminal formula here. Formula(11) is re-derived for our DECLARED
eta=8(1+S_m); the whole-suffix proof uses that declared bound. The

No conclusion about every horizon-scaled penalty, every retaining

# Variable-first-step comparison of the original positive-remainder tests

Status: complete joint mathematical DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4
round35, the first of the two analytical rounds authorized after completed34
by TASK.md and master_guidance4226374d0649bf55. No numerical evaluation,
solver, machine algebra, replay, formal verifier, consult or subagent is
used. The accepted mathematical inputs are facts04219554e41d9750 and
1252b8823bb5a290. Every additional ingredient used in this text is proved
here or reproduced as proof text in Appendix A, never assumed accepted.

No threshold seed is constructed. The result is an exact dominance
comparison on a variable-first-step domain, including the entire families
of calibrated inequalities specified below. It is not another version of
the former Bescape formula, and it does not assert that the remaining
older necessary system has a survivor.

## 1. Domain and the complete comparison statement

Throughout this document let

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v), a,b,c>0, bc<=1, sqrt(2)<=v<2,
    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,
    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, C=1+sum h=4+a+b+c+v,
    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t, gamma=w/mu,
    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t),
    R=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2,
    D=b^2*N^2-A^2,
    Q=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2,
    U=A^2-C^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2, V=Q/a^2.

Here R is the older repaired third-cut energy, Q is the older actual
unit-quadratic energy, and U is the older scalar-return energy. Section2
reproves all three as necessary consequences of the full original cone.
They are only THREE members of the complete older necessary system.
None of its other tests is discarded in the comparisons below.

Let H be the positive root

 H=(2*b*w-beta
     +sqrt((beta-2*b*w)^2+4*b*w^2*(1-b*t)/mu))/(2*(1-b*t)),
 k=H/(a^2*mu), k0=b^2*w/(ell*mu), eps=k*R-D.

Appendix A derives the original-array necessary conditions eps>=0,
the first-row calibration, its joint lifted-row family, and the
terminal/full-quadratic-column test R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)). It keeps all
twenty original multipliers, exact flow, and PSD on all five gradients.

The exact comparison conclusions are as follows.

1. On the ENTIRE domain above, R>=0, Q>=0 and U>=0 already imply
   eps>=0. They also imply the original terminal test
   R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)) wherever the older terminal cut makes Psi defined.
   Thus these two new tests exclude no survivor of the COMPLETE older
   conjunction on this variable-first-step domain.

2. On this domain with EITHER a>=sqrt(2)-1 OR D>=0, the same three
   old tests imply EVERY member of the original first-row calibration
   family (A.14), including its optimized capped value and all zero-energy
   cases. Both alternatives allow a variable first step.

3. On the concrete subdomain

     sqrt(2)-1<=a<1, ab<=1, c*(v-1)<=1,
     eta=b/(1+w+v), a*eta<1,

   retain in addition the COMPLETE older2948 first-row bound (7) below.
   Then every original joint family member (A.16), for EVERY mixing
   weight lambda>=0 and EVERY permitted price, is also implied.
   This is a comparison of the whole family, not a sampled price or
   a selected mixture. The capacity remains free; in particular the
   implication holds on its intersection with C>=193/25.

Intersecting any of these domains with every other retained cut,
clipping, adjacent/lifted triangle, C82/C84, C83/C86, endpoint or2948
condition preserves the implication. The proof does not use, or claim,
nonemptiness of that intersection. Consequently no full-old-system
survivor on the relevant domain can witness independence of the stated
new tests. Full original-array feasibility is not implied by this result.

## 2. The older inputs, with actual all-pair witnesses

The full original interface and primitive energy V_h are reproduced in
Appendix A.1 from accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10. For saturated
values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, direct expansion of the GD updates gives

    V_h=||x0||^2-||x4+s*g4||^2, s=C-1.                 (1)

Indeed the weighted value and gradient sums telescope to
||x0||^2-||x4||^2-2s*f4-s(s+1)||g4||^2, which is (1).
For any such data their ordered residual is
Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>.

For Q use the actual function f(x)=x^2/2, started at1. Its gradient
column is (1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1)), and x4=g4. Formula(1) gives Q.

For U use the actual one-sided clipped function

    f(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1; f(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1.

Its derivative is min(x,1), which is nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz;
its unique minimizer is0. Starting at A, the complete iterates are

    x=(A,1,-b,bc,-bc*(v-1)),
    g=(1,1,-b,bc,-bc*(v-1)).

The condition bc<=1 and v>=1 make every listed gradient correct.
All four original updates hold. Each value is saturated, so (1) gives U.

For R take the two scalar data arrays

    x_base=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), g_base=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    x_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abc,-abt), g_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abt,-abt).

Both obey the original updates. Form their orthogonal sum, with weights
ell and mu, and add the correspondingly weighted saturated values.
The complete nonzero finite residual list is

    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*(2+a+b),
    Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,
    Q03=-a*b*u*mu*v*t,
    Q23=-a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.                            (2)

All other ordered finite residuals vanish. This follows from the
normals (2+a+b,beta,0,-v*t,0) in the base and
(0,0,0,-ab*v*t,0) in the auxiliary. The sole potentially positive
auxiliary residual, Q13, cancels because ell=a^2*b*(1-bt).
The optimum comparisons also hold: Q_(i,star)=0, and the opposite
residuals are negatives of weighted <g_i,x_i-g_i>, each nonnegative.
In particular at index3 the latter product is
ell*v*t^2+mu*a^2*b^2*v*t^2>=0; at0 and1 it comes only from the
nonnegative base normal. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph2,
constructs one static smooth convex interpolant of these complete jets.
Equivalently, projection onto their gradient convex hull realizes them;
zero belongs to that hull because g3=-t*g2 in the orthogonal sum.
Their terminal position equals their terminal gradient, and (1) gives

    ell*N^2+mu-C^2*(ell+mu*a^2*b^2)*t^2=R,

using ell+mu*a^2*b^2=a^2*b*beta. Thus full feasibility forces

                          R>=0, Q>=0, U>=0.             (3)

These are established older tests, rederived here jointly. No minimum
radius normalization is used in this primitive-energy comparison.

The older terminal cut is

                  v<=2C/(C+1).                         (4)

For its actual witness start the function with derivative
clamp(x,[-(v-1),1]) at C-v=1+h0+h1+h2. Its gradients are1 at indices0,1,2,3,
and -(v-1) at4; the final position is -(v-1). Formula(1) gives
(C-v)^2-C^2*(v-1)^2>=0. Since C-v>0 and v>=1, this is exactly (4).
Appendix A independently derives it from the actual terminal row.

For the joint comparison in conclusion3 put

    delta=u^2-2, eta=b/(1+w+v),
    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,
    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2.

Its complete original identity, derived using the explicit lifted-cut
data in Appendix A.4, is

 Erow=nu*(m+a*(Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40))
       +(1+eta)^2*M11
       +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13),       (5)

where m=M00. The same original first row gives

 2*e0^T*M*pquad=u*(m-delta)
    +a^2*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30
                         +(1+bc*(v-1))*Lambda40).    (6)

Under ab<=1 and c*(v-1)<=1 each incoming coefficient in the last
parentheses is at most beta, and a*beta<=u. PSD Cauchy--Schwarz,
(5), and (6) therefore give precisely the retained old inequality

 Erow>=0,
 u*delta<=u*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Q*Erow/nu).                (7)

No incoming multiplier or Gram entry has been independently selected.
For delta>=0, solve the increasing quadratic in
sqrt(Erow/nu)>=0, keeping its nonnegative root. Since

    Qplus:=Q+u^2*delta=a^2*U,                          (8)

condition(7) implies

 Erow>=nu/u^2*(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.                (9)

At delta=0 the right side is0 and Erow>=0 suffices. This uses the
FULL old2948 inequality, rather than its weaker strict scalar corollary.

## 3. A positive decomposition in the older energies

Introduce new local comparison scalars

    zeta=b*mu/beta, kappa=zeta/H,
    xi=(H+1)/(H+gamma), theta0=1-xi^2,
    Y=C^2*b^2*t^2, Z=(v^2-1)^2.

The symbol zeta is local to this proof; it is not a bridge parameter.
All denominators are positive. Since b*t<1, one has0<zeta<1;
also gamma>1. The root equation for H is equivalent to

    H*(H+1)=zeta*(H+gamma)^2.

Consequently

    xi^2=zeta+kappa<1, theta0>0,
    U=A^2-Z*Y, V=1/a^2-Z*Y,
    Z>=1, Y>0.                                       (10)

Here xi<1 follows directly from gamma>1, not from a squared-root
selection. The other algebraic root of H is negative because the
constant term of its defining quadratic is negative; H is defined as
the positive one throughout. No matching or feasible root is dropped.

Direct expansion gives the following EXACT identities for every word
in the stated parameter domain, whether or not a multiplier array exists:

 kappa*eps=theta0*D+(1-zeta)*A^2+zeta/a^2-Y,

 (1-zeta)*eps
    =theta0*k*R+(1-zeta)*U+zeta*V+(Z-1)*Y.            (11)

To check the first, note kappa*k=b/(a^2*beta) and
(1-b*t)/beta=1-zeta. Multiplying R by this factor and subtracting
kappa*D yields
theta0*b^2*N^2+kappa*A^2+zeta/a^2-Y, as asserted.
For the second substitute D=kR-eps and theta0+kappa=1-zeta;
the last three terms equal (1-zeta)*A^2+zeta/a^2-Y.

Every term on the right of the second identity is nonnegative under
the old tests(3). This proves eps>=0 on the ENTIRE variable-a domain.
More precisely it supplies the two bounds used only for comparison:

 eps>=U+zeta*V/(1-zeta),
 R<=(1-zeta)*eps/(theta0*k).                         (12)

If v>sqrt(2), the term (Z-1)*Y is strictly positive, so eps>0.
If v=sqrt(2) and a!=sqrt(2)-1, the two numbers U and V have a
nonzero difference and cannot both vanish; hence eps>0 again.
No uniform lower margin as these parameters approach their boundaries
is asserted. This does not reopen the already closed matched locus.

## 4. The whole first-row calibration is dominated

Let sigma be the positive coefficient obtained from the actual original
row correction in Appendix A.3. There

    sigma=H*u^2/a^2
       -(H*u/a+qcorr/beta)^2/(1+H), qcorr>=0.

In particular

    0<sigma<=sigma0:=H*u^2/(a^2*(1+H)).              (13)

This follows by expansion; the subtracted square increases with qcorr.
The calibrated family is

 eps>=r*u*delta-r^2*Q/(sigma-r*u),
                  r<sigma/u, r<=r_cap,             (14)

where r_cap is the exact original sign cap in Appendix A.4. The price
r is distinct from R, rho, xi and theta0. Put

    S=sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q)=a*(sqrt(U)-sqrt(V)).

For ANY positive S0, the supremum over all real r<S0/u of

              r*u*delta-r^2*Q/(S0-r*u)

is

                       S0*S^2/u^2.                 (15)

For a direct proof substitute y=S0-r*u>0. The expression becomes

 S0*delta+2*S0*Q/u^2-Qplus*y/u^2-Q*S0^2/(u^2*y).

The inequality
Qplus*y+Q*S0^2/y>=2*S0*sqrt(Qplus*Q)
follows by squaring the difference of the two nonnegative square roots.
Its equality value, or its limit as y tends to0 or infinity, proves
(15). This retains Q=0, Qplus=0 and both zero. Restricting r by ANY
additional cap can only decrease the supremum. Thus it suffices to
prove eps>=sigma0*S^2/u^2.

First suppose a>=sqrt(2)-1. Then A^2>=1/a^2 and U>=V>=0. Hence

 sigma0*S^2/u^2
   =H/(1+H)*(sqrt(U)-sqrt(V))^2
   <=H/(1+H)*U<=eps,                                (16)

by (12). This proves EVERY (14), including every cap branch and every
zero-energy limit, on this variable-first-step domain. For a above the
endpoint, U-V>0, so U>0 and the last comparison is strict.

There is a second domain that does not order a relative to sqrt(2)-1.
If D>=0, subtract H/(1+H)*(sqrt(U)-sqrt(V))^2 from the first identity
in (11) and multiply as indicated. The result is exactly

 kappa*[eps-H/(1+H)*(sqrt(U)-sqrt(V))^2]
   =theta0*D+(Z-1)*Y
    +(1-zeta-zeta/(1+H))*U
    +zeta*H/(1+H)*V
    +2*zeta/(1+H)*sqrt(U*V).                        (17)

The U coefficient is theta0+kappa/(1+H)>0; every other term is
nonnegative. Thus eps>=sigma0*S^2/u^2, and hence the entire calibration
family, also holds on the stated D>=0 domain with no first-step ordering.

## 5. Every joint lifted-row price is dominated on the stated domain

Now impose exactly conclusion3's domain, including (7). For any
lambda>=0 the original joint family is

 eps+lambda*Erow
    >=r*u*delta-r^2*Q/(sigma+lambda*nu-r*u),          (18)

where r<(sigma+lambda*nu)/u and r satisfies the two actual incoming
coefficient caps in Appendix A.4. The mixing weight lambda here is a
scalar, not an original multiplier Lambda_ij.

Equations(9) and(16) give

 eps+lambda*Erow
    >=(sigma0+lambda*nu)*S^2/u^2
    >=(sigma+lambda*nu)*S^2/u^2.                     (19)

The unrestricted supremum formula(15), with
S0=sigma+lambda*nu>0, dominates the right side of EVERY permitted
(18). Thus all mixing weights and prices are handled simultaneously.
There is no exchange of independent original arrays: (5),(6) and the
Appendix A remainder use the same actual M and Lambda in the necessity
derivation. The dominance proof is then a step-only implication from
the complete old-system conditions. It asserts no converse to full PSD.

## 6. The entire terminal/full-column Psi condition is also dominated

The complete definition and original-row proof of Psi are reproduced
in Appendix A.5. To avoid treating a formula without a domain, impose
the old endpoint test(4); then

    nbar=s*(2C-(C+1)*v)/v>=0

and every square root defining Psi is real. Its constants satisfy

    Psi(sqrt(eps))>=Gcost*eps,
    Gcost>=Pcost>=ell*mu*beta/c1=1/(k-k0),
    c1=beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w)>0.                      (20)

All omitted terms of Psi are nonnegative; no original terminal
coefficient is assumed zero. Root positivity in Appendix A proves
k>k0. In the present comparison notation

    k0/k=kappa*gamma/(1-zeta).

Since gamma>1 and theta0=1-zeta-kappa,

    0<1-zeta-kappa*gamma<theta0,
    1/(k-k0)>(1-zeta)/(theta0*k).                    (21)

Combining(12),(20),(21) proves

                 R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)).                 (22)

Thus every old-system survivor on the full domain of Section1 already
passes the ENTIRE stated original terminal/full-quadratic-column test.
This conclusion does not assume D>=0 or a>=sqrt(2)-1. Its earlier
positive-root reformulation eps>=y0^2, when R>Psi(0), is equally
redundant: Psi is increasing and (22) is that same condition.

This is the quantitative terminal condition itself, not merely the
nonnegativity prerequisite eps>=0, and not Bescape. It still must not
be confused with matching the actual original terminal rows.

## 7. Boundaries, unresolved comparisons, and the remaining full cone

All steps in the stated domain are strictly positive. No zero-step
gradient has been deleted and no result outside that domain is inferred.
The boundary bc=1 is allowed in every displayed calculation: b*t<1
because v>0, so ell, zeta, kappa, theta0 and k-k0 stay positive.
At v=sqrt(2), Z=1 is retained exactly. Conditions Q=0, U=0, R=0,
D=0, nbar=0 and a=sqrt(2)-1 have been handled without division by any
of those energies or slacks. The sign caps remain finite on their
specified domains; formula(15) uses limits when the free supremum is
not attained. There is no hidden compactness margin.

The complete old-system comparison is now decided for eps and Psi on
all a>0 with sqrt(2)<=v<2, and for (14) and (18) on the precise
additional domains in Section1. In particular the whole newly calibrated
subsystem (eps, all first-row prices, all lambda mixtures, and Psi) adds
NO exclusion on conclusion3's variable-first-step domain. This says
nothing about whether the old conjunction is empty there.

The following distinctions remain essential.

* The terminal region0<v<sqrt(2) is outside (11)'s nonnegative
  decomposition because Z-1<0. No complete-old-system dominance or
  survivor is supplied there. The older h0=sqrt(2) restriction on v
  is not decided anew by this result. The exact check below shows
  that the terminal hypothesis cannot simply be deleted from the
  implication using just R,Q,U.
* For a<sqrt(2)-1 and D<0, (14) has not been compared on the whole
  domain. The proof of (16) uses the opposite U,V order, and (17)
  would have a negative D term. The lambda-mixture conclusion uses
  the retained full2948 inequality and exactly its stated hypotheses.
* No exact threshold h,Lambda,M is produced. For any surviving word
  the ORIGINAL twenty signs, exact flow and full5x5 PSD still must
  hold simultaneously. The remaining original row covariances and
  signs cannot be chosen separately from the energies in this proof.
  In particular (22) is only an implication obtained after several
  one-sided bounds on the actual terminal row. Its automatic validity
  supplies none of that row's missing coefficients.

This result removes a concrete family of prospective separating tests
on a substantial variable-step domain; it does not rename original
feasibility as a new solved subproblem. No fact has been submitted or
accepted. Accepted04219554e41d9750 still requires an actual seed of
length4 and sum at least168/25, equivalently C>=193/25. The original
exact-profile target remains open.

For a concrete counterexample to that HYPOTHESIS DELETION ONLY, let
alpha be the unique positive root of

                   alpha^2*(11+2*alpha)=1.

The left side has positive derivative for alpha>0, is0 at0, and is
35/27>1 at1/3; thus0<alpha<1/3. Take

           a=alpha, b=c=v=1, h=(1+alpha,2,2,1).

Direct substitution, with no numerical root evaluation, gives

    R=(3/2)*(1-alpha^2*(11+2*alpha))=0,
    Q=1, U=(2+alpha)^2>0,
    D=(4+alpha)^2-(2+alpha)^2=12+4*alpha>0,
    eps=k*R-D=-D<0.

Hence R>=0,Q>=0,U>=0 alone do not imply eps>=0 when the last-step
hypothesis is removed. This is NOT a survivor of the COMPLETE older
system, NOT a threshold example and NOT a primitive seed: its capacity
C=7+alpha<22/3<193/25. It is only a symbolic audit of the hypothesis
in our dominance proof. It supplies no independence conclusion in the
remaining task-relevant threshold region.

## Appendix A. Reproduced full original-row necessity arguments

The following is proof text reproduced from the earlier unverified
original-row draft, to be read and checked jointly with Sections1--7.
Its local equation numbers carry prefix A when cited in the main text.
It uses the same a,b,c,v,h,A,N,C,R,D,Q,u,beta,w,t,mu,ell,k,H parameters;
its Hbar equals H, its xi price is the main text's r, and its rstar,
rshift,rJB,rcoef are separate locally defined quantities. Its rho is
not the comparison xi of Section3. The older capped optimizer and
matched eps=0 analysis are intentionally not used in this comparison.

## 1. Original full interface and common identities

Let

    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v),  a,b,c>0, v>=0, bc<=1,
    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,
    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, Hpre=2+a+b, H0pre=1+a+b,
    s=sum h, C=1+s=4+a+b+c+v,
    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t,
    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t), z=a*b*v*t,
    R=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2,
    D=N^2*b^2-A^2.

Let e_i be the five original standard columns, indexed0 through4,
e=sum e_i, and P_i=sum_(j<i)h_j. Define

    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    Dmat=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, M=Dmat+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

The hypotheses are ALL twenty signs Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, zero
diagonal, L^T e=omega, and M>=0 on ALL of R^5. There is no support,
rank, symmetry, reversal, or duplicated-gradient restriction.

The accepted identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                                 -s(s+1)||g4||^2, (1)

on formal data with x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. Formal substitutions below
are used as polynomial identities; no unproved interpolation is assumed.

Te=e+P and the flow give e^TMe=0. Indeed e^TDmat e=-s^2-sum h_i^2
and omega^T(e+P)=s^2+sum h_i^2. Applying PSD to e+r*y for both
signs of the real number r gives Me=0. The same argument proves

    |<x,y>_M|^2<=||x||_M^2*||y||_M^2,
    ||x||_M^2=0 implies Mx=0,                           (2)

where <x,y>_M=x^TMy and ||x||_M^2=x^TMx. Under the flow, the
individual kernel equations are

    sum_j Lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i), i<4,
    sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2sC.                       (3)

These follow by expanding 2Me=2Dmat e+L(e+P)+T^T omega.

Put

    E=e0, J=e0+e1, B=e3+e4,
    m=||E||_M^2, S=||J||_M^2, Tcut=||B||_M^2, rJB=<J,B>_M,
    B0=Lambda30+Lambda40, B1=Lambda31+Lambda41,
    Avec=u*E-a*beta*J-a*b*mu*B,
    c03old=u*mu*z, c23old=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.

The three exact identities needed here are

    R=ell*mu^2*Tcut+mu*||Avec||_M^2
         +ell*mu*(Hpre*B0+beta*B1)+c03old*Lambda03+c23old*Lambda23, (4)

    D=beta*((b*w*H0pre-u)*B0+b^2*w*B1-2*b*w*rJB-beta*S
                     -u*Lambda20-b*v*c*(Lambda04+Lambda14)),       (5)

    2<E,B>_M=a*B0-Lambda03-(1+v)*Lambda04.                         (6)

To prove (4), use saturated values f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 on

    x_base=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), g_base=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    x_aux =(1,-a,ab,-abc,-abt), g_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abt,-abt).

For these values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j). In ell times the base
plus mu times the auxiliary, the complete nonzero residual list is

    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*Hpre, Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,
    Q03=-c03old, Q23=-c23old.

Every other ordered residual is zero. In particular Q13 cancels
because ell=a^2*b*(1-bt); at Q03 use ell+ab(1+abt)=ab*u, and
at Q23 use ell+a^2*b^2*mu=a^2*b*beta. Telescoping (1) gives
R; the two gradient columns modulo e are -mu*B and Avec, proving (4).

For (5), start the scalar two-step cut at x0=A with gradients
(1,1,-b), then extend FORMALLY with constant gradient -b and affine
values through the last two steps. Its energy is -D and its column
modulo e is beta*J. Its negative residuals are
Q20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta, and its positive residuals are
Q03=Q13=w*b*beta, Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta. All others vanish.
Thus (1) gives

 D=b*beta*(w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14))
                        -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).

Besides (6), direct multiplication of T gives

    2<e1,B>_M=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(1+v)*Lambda14.

Adding these rows, and using (w+v)-w(1+v)=-vc, gives (5).

The additional ORIGINAL row used this round is

    2<E,J+B>_M=-a*Lambda20+Lambda02+w*Lambda03+w*Lambda04.           (7)

In fact e2=e-J-B and
2M02=a*Lambda20-Lambda02-w*(Lambda03+Lambda04). This proves (7)
without any PSD quotient or new support assumption.


### Definition needed for the original positive root

For k>0 and real theta, set

    F(k,theta)=b^2*w^2-z*theta
          +(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta^2/4-k*ell*mu^2.

This definition and the original row identities just proved suffice for
the following derivation. No capped optimizer or eps=0 matching is used.

## 3. The unrestricted root and the new full-row remainder

Assume v>0 for now. Then ell>0, z>0 and 1-bt>0. Set

    k0=b^2*w/(ell*mu), kold=b^2*w^2/(ell*mu^2).

The minimum of F(k,theta) over all real theta is attained at

    theta(k)=2*z*k*mu/(1+a^2*k*mu).

It is strictly decreasing as a function of k: at each fixed theta,
partial F/partial k=-ell*mu^2-theta^2/(4*k^2*mu)<0, and the minimum
is attained continuously. At k0 the minimum divided by mu^2 is
b^2*(gamma-1)*(gamma+a^2*k0*mu)/(1+a^2*k0*mu)>0,
where gamma=w/mu>1. At kold it is negative, since F(kold,0)=0 and
the negative linear term improves it for small positive theta.
There is therefore a unique root k in (k0,kold).

Here is an explicit definition requiring no root search. Define

    Hbar=(2*b*w-beta
          +sqrt((beta-2*b*w)^2+4*b*w^2*(1-b*t)/mu))/(2*(1-b*t)),
    k=Hbar/(a^2*mu), theta=2*z*k*mu/(1+Hbar),
    x=b*w-a*theta/2=b*(w+Hbar*mu)/(1+Hbar), rho=x/beta,
    hcoef=theta/(2*k*mu), jcoef=a^2*theta/2,
    rstar=u*theta/2.

The positive Hbar solves

    (1-bt)*Hbar^2+(beta-2*bw)*Hbar-b*w^2/mu=0,

which follows by substituting theta(k) into F=0. Also

    0<rho<1, hcoef+jcoef=z,
    a*x-a*b*mu=hcoef, k*ell*mu^2+theta^2/(4*k*mu)
                                            =2*b*w*x-x^2.         (8)

The inequalities use mu<w and bw<=beta, with strictness supplied
by Hbar>0. The identities follow by expansion and F=0.

Full original feasibility itself forces v<2. Indeed the terminal
diagonal is n=||e4||_M^2=sum_(j<4)Lambda4j-s(s+2)>=0.
Every weight in the last row of (3) is at least v. Hence

    v*n<=s*(2C-(C+1)*v), so v<=2C/(C+1)<2.                       (9)

We now work on 0<v<2; v>=2 was already excluded by this original row.
Define the nonnegative correction

    qcorr=max(0,((1+v)*rstar-beta*b*v*c)
                         /(w+(1+v)*(1-rho))),
    rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho),
    X=beta*J+x*B.

The EXACT original-array remainder is

    eps:=k*R-D
       =(1+Hbar)*||X||_M^2
         -2*(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)*<E,X>_M+k*mu*u^2*m
         +cB*B0+c1*B1+c20*Lambda20+qcorr*Lambda02
         +c03*Lambda03+c04*Lambda04+c14*Lambda14+c23*Lambda23,      (10)

where

    cB=k*ell*mu*Hpre-beta*(b*w*H0pre-u)+a*rshift,
    c1=beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w), c20=beta*u-a*qcorr,
    c03=k*c03old-rshift+w*qcorr,
    c04=beta*b*v*c-(1+v)*rshift+w*qcorr,
    c14=beta*b*v*c, c23=k*c23old.

To check the identity, first combine (4) and (5). Its Gram part is

 beta^2*||J||_M^2+2*beta*b*w*<J,B>_M+k*ell*mu^2*Tcut
                                                 +k*mu*||Avec||_M^2.

Subtract 2*rshift*<E,B>_M+2*qcorr*<E,J+B>_M from that Gram part.
Restore them using (6) and (7); this gives precisely all costs in
(10). Since rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho), the subtraction differs
from 2*rstar*<E,B> by 2*qcorr*<E,X>/beta. F=0 and (8) turn the
uncorrected Gram into ||X||^2+k*mu*||uE-aX||^2. This proves (10).

Every displayed cost is nonnegative; cB,c1,c20,c03,c14,c23 are
STRICTLY positive. Here is the full sign and PSD check.

Let

    qmax=beta*u*(sqrt(k*mu*(1+Hbar))-a*k*mu).

Then

    0<=qcorr<qmax<beta*u/(2a).                                  (11)

If qcorr=0 this follows from Hbar>0. Otherwise put
tau=sqrt(Hbar/(1+Hbar)) in (0,1), Vprod=vbc, and
Sden=2+b+c+v. Direct substitution gives

    qcorr=(beta/a)*Vprod*(u*tau^2-a*beta)
                              /(Sden-Vprod+Vprod*tau^2),
    qmax=(beta*u/a)*tau/(1+tau).

The numerator of qcorr is positive in this case. The inequality
qcorr<qmax is equivalent to

    Vprod*(1+tau)*(u*tau-a*beta)<u*tau*Sden.

Its left side is smaller than 2*Vprod*u*tau. Moreover
2*Vprod<=2v<2+b+c+v=Sden, using bc<=1 and v<2. This proves the
strict inequality. The upper bound on qmax follows from
tau/(1+tau)<1/2.

Also rshift>0: when qcorr>0 its numerator is less than
(1+v)*rstar, while its denominator is strictly larger than
(1+v)*(1-rho). Thus qcorr<rstar/(1-rho).
At k=k0 the part of cB before a*rshift equals u*(1-bc)>=0;
it strictly increases with k. Hence cB>0. Since k>k0, c1>0.
Equation (11) gives c20>beta*u/2>0. Finally
rstar<k*u*mu*z=k*c03old and rshift<=rstar, proving c03>0.
The definition of qcorr gives c04>=0, and c14,c23>0.

Set

    alpha=(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)/(1+Hbar), Y=X-alpha*E,
    sigma=k*mu*u^2-(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)^2/(1+Hbar)>0.

The positivity of sigma is exactly qcorr<qmax. Thus (10) is

    eps=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)*||Y||_M^2+the nonnegative costs in (10).  (12)

In particular every full original certificate obeys D<=kR. This
necessity argument uses the actual original rows. Its comparison with
the complete older conditions is supplied by Sections1--7 of the
present joint text, not by a claim about an auxiliary optimizer.

## 4. The actual quadratic column and joint first-row budget

Define

    pquad=(1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1))^T,
    Q=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2,
    delta=u^2-2, W0=1+bc*(v-1).

Tp=e and Le=0. Telescoping h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 gives

    ||pquad||_M^2=Q>=0,
    2<E,pquad>_M=u*(m-delta)
             +a^2*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40).    (13)

For the row formula, let O0=sum_(j>0)Lambda0j and
I0=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40. Then m=1-a^2-aO0 and node0 flow
gives Lambda10=O0+2u-I0. With Te0=u e-a e0 and
Mp=Dmat pquad+T^T L^T pquad/2 one obtains

 pquad^T L e0=uO0+2au-a*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40),
 2<E,pquad>_M=2(1-a^2)-a*pquad^T L e0,

which gives (13). Both incoming weights 1-bc and W0 are nonnegative,
and 1-bc<=W0, including v<1.

By (12), eps>=sigma*m+c20*Lambda20+cB*B0. Define

    xi_cap=min(c20/(a^2*beta), cB/(a^2*W0)),

omitting the second term if W0=0. For every real xi with
xi<=xi_cap and xi<sigma/u,

    eps>=xi*u*delta-xi^2*Q/(sigma-xi*u).                         (14)

Subtract xi*u*delta using (13). All remaining multiplier coefficients
are nonnegative: for xi>=0 this is the definition of xi_cap and the
order of the incoming weights; for xi<0 the coefficients increase.
The remaining Gram expression after adding xi^2Q/(sigma-xi*u) is

    ||sqrt(sigma-xi*u)*E+xi*pquad/sqrt(sigma-xi*u)||_M^2>=0.

Thus this is the same ORIGINAL matrix and column, not independent
energy selections. Let Qplus=Q+u^2*delta. Sending xi to -infinity
shows Qplus>=0, and

    Qplus=a^2*(A^2-C^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2).

That prerequisite is the old scalar-return condition and is not
counted as an independent improvement.

For completeness, when Q,Qplus>0 the free maximizing xi is
xi_free=(sigma/u)*(1-sqrt(Q/Qplus)). If xi_free<=xi_cap, (14)
has supremum

    Bcal=(sigma/u^2)*(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.

Otherwise its supremum is

    Bcal=xi_cap*u*delta-xi_cap^2*Q/(sigma-xi_cap*u).

The latter case automatically has xi_cap<sigma/u. For Q>0,Qplus=0,
the supremum is sigma*Q/u^2. For Q=0,Qplus>=0 it is
u*delta*min(xi_cap,sigma/u), and it is zero when Q=delta=0.
These statements follow on putting y=sigma-xi*u>0: the objective is

 sigma*delta+2*sigma*Q/u^2-(delta+Q/u^2)*y-Q*sigma^2/(u^2*y).

The earlier first-row budget is also retained, on its stated domain
0<a<1 and a*eta<1, where

    eta=b/(1+w+v), nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,
    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2.

Its exact identity is

 Erow=nu*(m+a*(Lambda20+B0))+(1+eta)^2*M11
                 +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13).        (15)

Here is a direct derivation. For orthonormal physical vectors U,V set
gfirst=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V and use saturated values on

 Xphys=(U+u*gfirst,U,-b*U,-(1+v)*eta*U,-eta*U),
 Gphys=(gfirst,U,-eta*U,-eta*U,-eta*U).

The nonzero residuals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-a*nu,
Q12=-(1+eta)*(b-eta), and Q13=-(1+eta)*v*eta. Every other finite
residual is zero. Use ||gfirst||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a and
<U,gfirst>+a||gfirst||^2=1-a*eta to check the target0 residuals.
The energy telescope gives Erow and the two gradient columns modulo
e are sqrt(nu)*E and (1+eta)*e1, proving (15).

In particular, for EVERY lambda>=0, (14) holds with its left side
eps+lambda*Erow, sigma replaced by sigma+lambda*nu, and cap replaced
by

 min((c20+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*beta),
                         (cB+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*W0)).              (16)

This follows by adding the TWO exact budgets before using (13), so
the same m and original incoming coefficients must pay for both.
It does not replace any of the older2948 conditions. For example,
putting Wrow=max(beta,W0), Krow=max(u,a*Wrow) in (13),(15),(2) gives

 u*delta<=Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Q*Erow/nu).

For delta>0, v>1, ab<=1 and c(v-1)<=1, Krow=u and Q<1. These imply
Erow>nu*delta^2/u^2 and

    b<(a+3)*(C-a-2)/((a+1)*(C+a+2)).

To see the latter equivalence, the direct factorization is

 u^2*(Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2)
    =((a+3)-(delta+u*C)*eta)*((a+3)+(u*C-delta)*eta).

The second factor is positive since C>A and u*A>delta. Substituting
eta=b/(C-A-b) in the positive first factor gives the displayed
bound. All its hypotheses remain.

## 5. A further matched full-column inequality near equality

In this section v>0 and all constants are the ones already defined.
Let

    dphys=a*b*c*v=(1+v)*z,
    nbar=s*(2C-(C+1)*v)/v>=0,
    rcoef=ell*mu^2+mu*hcoef^2>0,
    Lnorm=sqrt((u-a*alpha)^2/sigma+a^2/(1+Hbar)),
    Lterm=sqrt((alpha-beta)^2/sigma+1/(1+Hbar)),
    Kterm=(dphys/x)*Lterm*sqrt(nbar), Jfee=a/(2*x),
    Pcost=max(ell*mu*Hpre/cB,ell*mu*beta/c1,c03old/c03,1/k),
    Gcost=max(mu*Lnorm^2,Pcost).

All denominators are strictly positive. If nbar<0, the original
terminal budget (9) already excludes the word; it is never square-rooted.

Every full original array satisfies the following explicit scalar test.
Write y=sqrt(eps), which requires eps>=0, and set

 tau_max(y)=(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y
       +sqrt((sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)^2+4*jcoef*(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)))
                                                            /(2*jcoef),
 Psi(y)=rcoef*tau_max(y)^2
            +2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*tau_max(y)*y+Gcost*y^2.

Then

                              R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)).                 (17)

Here is the full derivation retaining the actual quadratic column.
The vector identity, checked in all five coordinates, is

    pquad=uE-aX-jcoef*B+dphys*e4+ab*e.

The original terminal rows give

    2<E,e4>_M=a*Lambda40-Lambda04,
    2<J,e4>_M=2<E,e4>_M+beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14.

Since X=Y+alpha E, they imply

 x<B,e4>_M=(alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M
                              -beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/2.

Taking the inner product of pquad with B therefore gives

 <B,pquad>_M=(u-a*alpha)<E,B>_M-a<Y,B>_M-jcoef*Tcut
       +(dphys/x)*((alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M)
       -dphys*beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/(2*x).       (18)

Let eps_G=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)||Y||_M^2 and let eps_C be the cost sum
in (12). Thus eps=eps_G+eps_C with both nonnegative. Weighted
Cauchy--Schwarz gives

 ||(u-a*alpha)E-aY||_M<=Lnorm*sqrt(eps_G),
 ||(alpha-beta)E+Y||_M<=Lterm*sqrt(eps_G).

Also ||e4||_M^2<=nbar by (9), and
c14=beta*dphys/a. Drop the negative Lambda40,Lambda41 terms in
(18), use Lambda14<=eps_C/c14, and use
<B,pquad>_M>=-sqrt(Tcut*Q). The result is

 jcoef*Tcut<=sqrt(Tcut*Q)
        +(Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut)+Kterm)*sqrt(eps_G)+Jfee*eps_C.          (19)

For the other direction (4) and Avec=(u-a*alpha)E-aY+hcoef*B give

 R<=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps_G)
                                    +mu*Lnorm^2*eps_G+Pcost*eps_C
  <=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps)+Gcost*eps.           (20)

The cost bound follows by comparing each cost in (4) to the cost of
the SAME multiplier in (12); their maximum ratio is precisely Pcost.
Thus costs and energies have not been chosen from different arrays.
Replacing eps_G,eps_C separately by eps in (19) gives, for
tcut=sqrt(Tcut)>=0,

 jcoef*tcut^2-(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)*tcut-(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)<=0.

Its nonnegative root is tau_max(y). The right side of (20) increases
with tcut>=0, which proves (17).

This supplies a quantified positive-gap exclusion test. Psi is
continuous and strictly increasing on [0,infinity), tends to infinity,
and

    Psi(0)=rcoef*Q/jcoef^2.

Consequently, if R>rcoef*Q/jcoef^2, there is a unique y0>0 with
Psi(y0)=R, and every full array must have

                           eps>=y0^2>0.                         (21)

Strict increase follows from the explicit nondecreasing positive
coefficients in tau_max and Gcost>0. This is an algebraic definition
and proof; no numerical root finding is used. In particular Q=0,R>0
forces a positive eps, even when delta=0 and the first-row bound
(14) alone is zero. This prices a further original cross column.
No uniform lower bound as v,ell,R,Q or other factors vanish is claimed.
