# Max43: a paid whole-word gradient lift and its exact Huber ceiling

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No new assertion in
this manuscript is a fact-graph node. This is the second and final round
allocated by a23831f2a36b3c4a after completed41: completed ceiling43,
attempted ceiling44 including one historical interruption. The outer
loop, not this manuscript, records actual completion after return.

The original target remains the all-nonnegative-word, all-sufficiently-
large-horizon relation W_n^*/U_n^ZJ -> 1, or a certified asymptotic
full-class upper improvement. W=2R. Neither is established here.

Accepted inputs, read in the fact graph:

* 62a7d9329121fd75: the finite triangle and its projection construction.
* 1252b8823bb5a290: the original directed interpolation convention and
  its necessity/sufficiency, with the original GD normalization.
* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: the COMPLETE alternating-ray reference.
* dfde2fa326c908bf: the FULL complex-polynomial reference, including its
  specified minimizer, denominator, all cycles and maximal values.
* b98bb807d514e80d: complete K and the ORIGINAL benchmark recurrence.

Prior drafts102c186f5eaa6782,24e9d97b652efae8,4abd9088498f5cc5 and
43e339836c86f286 are research context, not premises. Every new ingredient
used below is proved within this joint draft. No formal gate is allowed.

## 1. Exact scope and conclusions

The changed state is an explicit common-coordinate lift of all three
triangle gradients. Its initial height depends on the entire physical
word. The resulting ONE static function has a different suffix, a
nontrivial minimum cone, nonminimum residuals, and dependent residual
increments. All of those changes are paid. This is not a continuation
that retains the old absolute triangle jets.

For ANY finite nonnegative prefix r of total mass S, followed by the
physical suffix(2,2,1), the complete declared family has exact supremum

    max{ 3/[13(S+1)^2], 1/(2S+11) }.                  (1)

The family is specified by actual compact convex sets, trajectories and
scalar parameters below, not by an unknown feasible Gram matrix. Formula
(1) includes all its parameters, all its legal boundary cases, arbitrary
isometric embeddings, and any finite orthogonal sum of its members on
the same word. The first term is attained by the planar degeneration.
The second is attained by the scalar degeneration and is approached by
explicit finite parameters with a nondegenerate triangle.

In particular, once S>=1 this entire family is bounded by the same-word
Huber witness, hence by complete K and complete F_ray. It cannot provide
a new stronger lower-bound mechanism on such prefixes. This closes only
the family declared here, not arbitrary interpolants or suffix redesigns.

Nevertheless there is a real change from the fixed-triangle state. An
explicit nondegenerate member, with all parameters chosen as functions
of S, has value V_lift satisfying, for S>=1,

    1/(2S+12) <= V_lift < 1/(2S+11),
    V_lift / [1/(2S+11)] -> 1 as S -> infinity.        (2)

Its terminal gap divided by the squared gradient entering the suffix
grows linearly with S. Thus the previous fixed-jet quadratic loss does
not apply: its retained-jet hypothesis has actually been removed.

On the actual mixed words

    h_m(u,c)=((u,c)^m,2,2,1),
    m>=1, 0<=u<=1, 1<c<=3/2,
    S=m(u+c), n=2m+3,                                (3)

the complete same-word ray and K references are exactly1/(2S+11).
For S>=400000 the FULL complex-polynomial reference is strictly below
the finite nondegenerate member in(2). Every physical query, including
u=0, u=1 and the final step1, is retained. The threshold is an analytic
convenient bound, not the output of a numerical evaluation.

For the ORIGINAL B_n, B_n V_lift tends to infinity uniformly over u,c
in(3). The zero-lift planar member instead has B_n V_planar -> 0 on
those same words. The improved order is therefore real, but it merely
recovers a witness already present in complete K. It gives no new
all-word profile theorem. No claim about the original H_m is made, and
none of its enclosing-m parameters is changed.

## 2. Explicit static function and all legal parameters

All vector spaces, norms and inner products are real Euclidean. Use the
accepted planar vectors

    p=p_ft=(1,2sqrt(3)), q=q_ft=(-2,-sqrt(3)),
    e=e_ft=(1,0), nu=(1,-sqrt(3)).

Thus ||p||^2=13, ||q||^2=7, ||e||^2=1 and

    p+2q+3e=0.                                       (4)

The bare p in this manuscript is this vector; the crude benchmark
exponent is denoted p_bd=log_2(3) to avoid a collision.

Choose a,b>=0, not both zero, and a finite real H with

    H>=1, a^2+b^2(H-6)>=0.                           (5)

If b=0, H is immaterial and may be chosen to be1. If a=0,b>0,
condition(5) becomes H>=6. In R^3=R^2 x R define

    P=(a p,b), Q=(a q,b), E=(a e,b),
    C=conv{0,P,Q,E},
    F(x)=max_(v in C){<x,v>-||v||^2/2}.               (6)

The prefix is r=(r_0,...,r_(k-1)), each r_j>=0. Put

    S=sum_(j=0)^(k-1) r_j,
    T_j=sum_(l=j)^(k-1) r_l, 0<=j<=k,
    x_j=((1+T_j)a p,(H+T_j)b), g_j=P.                (7)

In particular T_0=S and T_k=0. The empty prefix k=0 is allowed.
After it the four suffix samples, including the shared entry sample,
are

    label   position                 gradient       value
    A       (a p,Hb)                 P              13a^2/2+b^2(H-1/2)
    B       (-a p,(H-2)b)            Q               9a^2/2+b^2(H-5/2)
    C2      (3a e,(H-4)b)            E               5a^2/2+b^2(H-9/2)
    D       (2a e,(H-5)b)            E               3a^2/2+b^2(H-11/2).
                                                               (8)

The set C in(6) is unrelated to the label C2. Each prefix sample at
remaining mass T has value

    f(T)=13a^2(T+1/2)+b^2(H+T-1/2).                  (9)

Every claimed value will be justified by the actual projection. The
updates already have the correct algebraic form:

    x_j-r_j P=x_(j+1),
    (a p,Hb)-2P=(-a p,(H-2)b),
    (-a p,(H-2)b)-2Q=(3a e,(H-4)b),
    (3a e,(H-4)b)-E=(2a e,(H-5)b).                  (10)

The middle equality uses -p-2q=3e from(4). There is no virtual holding
step, compressed schedule, change of function, or reset between blocks.

### 2.1 Global convexity, smoothness and projection criterion

The maximum in(6) is finite because C is compact. Completing the square
identifies its unique maximizer as the Euclidean projection Pi_C(x).
For any v in C, the exact projection criterion is

    g=Pi_C(x) iff <x-g,v-g><=0 for all v in C.         (11)

Necessity follows by expanding squared distance along the segment from
g to v and taking its one-sided derivative at g. Conversely,
||x-v||^2-||x-g||^2=||v-g||^2-2<x-g,v-g>>=0, so(11) is sufficient.
The two projection inequalities at x,x', tested on each other's
projections and added, give

    ||Pi_C(x)-Pi_C(x')||^2
       <=<x-x',Pi_C(x)-Pi_C(x')>.

Thus Pi_C is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing inequalities in(6) then give

    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<Pi_C(x),x'-x>
       <=<Pi_C(x')-Pi_C(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

It follows that F is differentiable with gradient Pi_C. It is convex
as a maximum of affine functions of x. Thus F is a single everywhere
finite convex1-smooth function. This proves the required generalization
of the finite projection construction directly.

### 2.2 All old/new, new/new and minimum inequalities

For any two specified jets use exactly the accepted orientation

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Every value in(8)-(9) has f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Writing y_j=x_j-g_j
and expanding therefore gives

    Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                              (12)

All nonminimum gradients have common final coordinate b, so their
differences have zero final coordinate. The vertical residuals do not
disappear from the minimum rows; they disappear only from rows between
two nonminimum samples.

The nonminimum suffix matrix, in row/column order(A,B,C2,D), is

    a^2 [ 0 -6  0  0;
          0  0 -6 -3;
          0  0  0  0;
          0  0  0  0 ].                             (13)

For a prefix sample of remaining mass T, all its outgoing rows to
(A,B,C2,D) are a^2(0,-6,0,0). The four reverse rows from(A,B,C2,D)
to it are

    a^2(0,-21T,-12T,-12T).                           (14)

All ordered rows between two prefix samples are zero. These formulas
include the shared entry T=0 and every zero step. To check the constants
in(13)-(14), the unscaled planar residuals of(A,B,C2,D) are0,nu,2e,e, and

    <nu,p-q>=-6, <nu,e-q>=0,
    <2e,q-e>=-6, <e,q-e>=-3,
    <p,q-p>=-21, <p,e-p>=-12.

Include the zero minimum jet x_*=g_*=f_*=0. Every Q_(i,*) is0. The
reverse rows are the negatives of the following complete list of
residual-gradient products, denoted d_i:

    prefix T: d(T)=13a^2 T+b^2(H+T-1),
    A:        d_A=b^2(H-1),
    B:        d_B=a^2+b^2(H-3),
    C2:       d_C2=2a^2+b^2(H-5),
    D:        d_D=a^2+b^2(H-6).                      (15)

They are all nonnegative under(5). In fact d_B=d_D+3b^2,
d_C2=d_D+a^2+b^2, and d(T)=d_A+T(13a^2+b^2).
When b>0, d_A>=0 and d_D>=0 are also necessary for the declared
jets to have0 as a minimizer. They give precisely(5). When b=0 the
height is absent and every row is legal; fixing H=1 is just a convention.

Equations(13)-(15) prove(11) at every proposed sample: it suffices to
test v in the four vertices0,P,Q,E, and these are exactly the rows just
listed, in the corresponding column. Thus the proposed gradients are
the actual projections. Substitution into(6) gives exactly(8)-(9).
All finite values and every directed row are supplied. No boundary
condition or nonterminal row is dropped.

## 3. Exact minimum set and actual closest-minimizer cost

The exact minimum set of(6) is the polar cone

    Z={z in R^3:<z,P><=0,<z,Q><=0,<z,E><=0},
    min F=0.                                         (16)

Indeed,0 belongs to C, so F>=0. For z in Z, every inner product with
v in C is nonpositive, so F(z)=0. For z outside Z choose v in C with
<z,v>>0. The point eta v belongs to C for0<=eta<=1. For sufficiently
small eta>0, eta<z,v>-eta^2||v||^2/2>0, proving F(z)>0.

This identifies ALL minima, rather than assuming the old minimum stays
unique. For b>0 the negative vertical ray is contained in Z, so its
minimum set really has changed. For any z in Z, the rows from a sampled
jet to that minimum are Q_(i,z)=<z,g_i><=0, while the reverse rows are
Q_(z,i)=-d_i<=0. Between any two minima both rows are0. Thus arbitrary
minima, not just0, have both row orientations covered.

Despite the larger minimum set,0 is the exact closest minimizer to the
initial point x_0 in(7). Put theta=(S+1)/(S+H), which belongs to(0,1]
by H>=1. Relation(4) implies0 belongs to conv{p,q,e}; therefore theta p
belongs to that triangle, and

    v0=(a theta p,b) belongs to conv{P,Q,E},
    x_0=(S+H)v0.                                    (17)

For every z in Z, (17) gives <x_0,z><=0. Consequently

    ||x_0-z||^2=||x_0||^2+||z||^2-2<x_0,z>
       >=||x_0||^2,

with equality at z=0, and only there. Hence the true squared radius is

    R0^2=dist(x_0,Z)^2
        =13a^2(S+1)^2+b^2(S+H)^2.                    (18)

The actual normalized terminal payoff, in the project W=2R convention,
is exactly

    V(a,b,H;S)=[3a^2+b^2(2H-11)]
                 /[13a^2(S+1)^2+b^2(S+H)^2].         (19)

The denominator is positive. The terminal value is positive as well:
f_D=||E||^2/2+d_D, with d_D>=0 and E nonzero. No normalization by a
farther convenient minimizer is substituted for the true distance.

For general L>0 use L F and the physical GD steps h_i/L. All positions
and the normalized payoff stay unchanged. A common distance rescaling
and a translation or isometric embedding also preserve(19).

### 3.1 What old hypotheses have actually been removed

For b>0,H>1, each prefix residual has d(T)>0, so it is not a minimum:
its inner product with P is positive, contrary to(16). In particular
the suffix-entry residual(0,b(H-1)) is a nonminimum. Residual increments
inside the prefix are parallel to P, since

    y(T)-y(T')=(T-T')P.

They are genuinely dependent whenever two nonzero increments occur.
Their direction also has a component in the original triangle plane
when a>0. The old fresh-orthogonal and minimum-residual hypotheses both
fail. The specified conjugate family also changes from a maximum of
minimum planes to the explicit compact gradient domain(6).

Most decisively, ALL suffix absolute jets have changed: their gradients
now have coordinate b, their positions have a paid vertical history,
their values include the vertical terms in(8), and their minimum set
is(16). The old cancellation becomes

    P+2Q+3E=(0,6b),

so it no longer forces the old planar minimum rigidity. This is a real
whole-word suffix redesign. It is not an evasion of a theorem whose
fixed-jet hypotheses are still satisfied.

## 4. Exact closure of the entire declared family

Put J=2S+11 and D0=13(S+1)^2. For every finite real H,

    (S+H)^2-J(2H-11)=(H-S-11)^2>=0.                (20)

Let M=max{3/D0,1/J}. From(20),
3a^2<=M D0 a^2 and
b^2(2H-11)<=M b^2(S+H)^2. The second inequality remains valid when
2H-11 is negative. Adding proves V(a,b,H;S)<=M for every legal
parameter in(5), including all boundary cases.

The planar degeneration b=0,a>0 attains3/D0. The scalar degeneration
a=0,b>0,H=S+11 is legal since H>=11; it attains1/J. If one insists on
a>0 and b>0, take a=1,H=S+11 and let b increase without bound. Formula
(19) tends to1/J. Letting b decrease to0 with the same height approaches
3/D0. This proves the exact supremum(1), not just a one-sided estimate.

The more informative exact deficit identity is

    R0^2-J[3a^2+b^2(2H-11)]
       =a^2(13S^2+20S-20)+b^2(H-S-11)^2.             (21)

For S>=1, the first parenthesis is positive: it is13 at S=1 and its
derivative26S+20 is positive there and thereafter. Thus every member
with a>0 has V<1/J, irrespective of how a,b,H depend on the WHOLE word.
Optimizing heights, increasing dimension through embeddings, or making
the lift arbitrarily large cannot give a strict gain over Huber.

This exact classification also includes finite orthogonal sums on the
same word. Such a sum has the product minimum set, squared distance
equal to the sum of the component squared distances, and terminal
objective equal to the sum of the component objectives. Its normalized
payoff is therefore a weighted average of the component payoffs with
nonnegative squared-distance weights. It cannot exceed(1). This closes
that specified way of letting dimension grow with the horizon as well.
It does not close interacting lifts with different coupled trajectories.

The restriction responsible for the ceiling is now explicit. The
additional coordinate has the SAME gradient at every query. Its
terminal contribution and initial radius obey(20), exactly the scalar
Huber price, while all transverse suffix rows retain the fixed triangle
differences. The global function need not be an orthogonal sum, but its
entire declared transcript has this paid decomposition. For H<11/2 its
vertical terminal contribution is negative; the proof still covers it,
so positivity of separate contributions is not being assumed.

## 5. A whole-word rule that escapes the fixed-jet rate loss

For every S>=0 choose the finite NONDEGENERATE parameters

    a=1, b=S+1, H=S+11.                              (22)

They satisfy(5). Its exact normalized payoff is

    V_lift(S)=[3+(S+1)^2 J]
                 /[(S+1)^2(13+J^2)], J=2S+11.       (23)

For S>=1, (21) gives V_lift<1/J. On the other side,

    V_lift>=J/(13+J^2)
           =1/[J+13/J]>=1/(J+1)=1/(2S+12),           (24)

because J>=13. This proves(2) by multiplying by J and squeezing between
J/(J+1) and1. The convergence is uniform across words with S increasing;
it depends only on the exact total mass, with no uncounted prefix step.

For the actual entry into the redesigned suffix,

    ||g_A||^2=13+(S+1)^2,
    F(x_D)/||g_A||^2
       =[3+(S+1)^2(2S+11)]/[2(13+(S+1)^2)].          (25)

For S>=3, (S+1)^2>=13, so the ratio in(25) is at least
(2S+11)/4>=S/2. This is the explicit terminal-gap/entry-gradient growth
absent from the fixed triangle. It is not produced by a uniform scaling
of an unchanged old instance. Both relative gradient geometry and entry
height have changed with S, and their radius is(18).

This is a repeated legal rule with the correct order on long stable
prefixes. Equation(21) nevertheless proves that it is only a calibration
of the already available Huber strength. That distinction remains true
even though it will beat the complete complex-polynomial reference.

## 6. Complete same-word ray and K on the mixed words

Use the physical words(3), whose total mass is S+5. Recall the complete
accepted ray semantics. Choose any ordered checkpoint subset; write
the word as(block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(l-1),block_l), with s_i the sum
of block_i. For l>=1 put m_0=1/(1+s_0), O_0=0 and

    m_(i+1)=((b_i-1)m_i-O_i)/(1+s_(i+1)),
    O_(i+1)=O_i+s_i m_i.

Feasibility requires m_i>0 and m_i>=m_(i-2) when i>=2, and at the end

    z=(b_(l-1)-1)m_(l-1)-O_(l-1)
       >=(1+s_l)m_old,
    m_old=0 if l=1, m_old=m_(l-2) otherwise.

The terminal value maximizes
2e_term+2q_ray z-(2s_l+1)q_ray^2 over
q_ray in[m_old,z/(1+s_l)], where q_ray is the terminal ray magnitude and
e_term=sum_(i<l, i congruent l mod2)s_i m_i^2. The no-checkpoint case
has exact optimized value1/[1+2(S+5)]. These definitions retain the
remote offsets and all accumulated areas, exactly as in the fact.

On(3), all steps are at most2. For a feasible checkpoint sequence,
O_i>=0 and m_(i+1)<=m_i. If at least two checkpoints are chosen and
the last physical step1 is a checkpoint, then z<=0, while the required
right side is positive. If that final step is not a checkpoint, then
s_l>=1 and

    z<=(b_(l-1)-1)m_(l-1)<=m_(l-1)<=m_(l-2),

whereas the required value is at least2m_(l-2). Thus EVERY multiple-
checkpoint set is infeasible. This includes any attempted zero or unit
checkpoint. No accumulated area can rescue an empty terminal interval.

It remains to check EVERY singleton, which by the accepted definition
has value(h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)]. Here P_j,T_j are the actual
preceding and following masses. For a prefix step h_j<=3/2 the numerator
is at most1/4, and

    (1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j)>=1+2(P_j+T_j)
       =1+2(S+5-h_j)>=2S+8.

Its value is below1/(2S+11), since2S+11<4(2S+8). The singleton at the
first suffix2 has denominator7(S+1)^2, and

    7(S+1)^2-(2S+11)=7S^2+12S-4>0 for S>=1.

The singleton at the second suffix2 has denominator3(S+3)^2, larger
than2S+11 because the difference is3S^2+16S+16>0. The final step1 has
zero singleton value. Since S=m(u+c)>m>=1, these cases prove exactly

    F_ray_n(h_m)=K_n(h_m)=1/(2S+11).                  (26)

Combining(21),(23),(26), every nondegenerate member of the ENTIRE family
is strictly below this complete reference, and its supremum equals the
reference. The explicit finite rule(22) approaches it uniformly. This
is stronger than checking one checkpoint, one parameter or one prefix.

## 7. FULL complex-polynomial reference and a uniform separation

For completeness, use exactly the reference in dfde2fa326c908bf. For a
given length-n word h and z=alpha+i beta, let
Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z),
with xi_*=gamma_*=0. The complex plane has real inner product
<u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). Put kappa=alpha-|z|^2. For an ordered cycle C of
distinct indices in{*,0,...,n}, including length2 cycles, define

    Area_C=sum_(j->i in C) Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i),
    Energy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2.

The FULL legal domain D_h requires
kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C| for EVERY such cycle. With
q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2, let ell_i be the
maximum q-weight of a directed path i->*, allowing the empty path at*.
The maximal legal normalized terminal value is Gap_h(z)=-2ell_n.
It retains initial point1, minimum0 and denominator1. The accepted bound
we use is

    0<=Gap_h(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2.          (27)

No renormalization about a closer minimum is part of this reference.
The argument below bounds the full D_h using necessary cycles. It never
replaces D_h by the smaller universal lens.

### 7.1 A necessary small-curvature constraint from a physical suffix cycle

The optimum/initial two-cycle forces kappa>=0. Thus, writing A_sp=|z|^2,

    0<=A_sp<=alpha<=1.                               (28)

If the prefix polynomial at the start of the actual suffix(2,2,1)
vanishes, then the terminal polynomial vanishes and Gap_h(z)=0. Otherwise
divide the cycle through the optimum, the suffix entry, and the next
query by the positive squared modulus of that prefix polynomial. Its
positions become(0,1,w), where

    w=1-2z=d+i v, d=1-2alpha, v=-2beta,
    sigma=|w|^2=1-4alpha+4A_sp.

Its area is v and its energy is2(1+sigma-d). Therefore its necessary
cycle inequality is

    2v^2<=(1-sigma)(1+sigma-d).

Substituting v,d,sigma and using beta^2=A_sp-alpha^2 gives the exact
equivalent scalar condition

    (3-6alpha)A_sp+4A_sp^2<=alpha.                    (29)

In particular, for0<=alpha<=1/100,

    A_sp<=alpha/(3-6alpha)<=50alpha/147.              (30)

All cycles above use distinct physical indices, even if some positions
coincide. No unqueried inserted point is used besides the allowed
optimum sample.

### 7.2 All complex parameters on every mixed word

Every prefix step r in(3) lies in[0,3/2]. In the regime alpha<=1/100,
(30) gives

    |1-rz|^2=1-2r alpha+r^2 A_sp
       <=1-(73/49)r alpha
       <=exp[-(73/49)r alpha].                       (31)

The first upper bound is nonnegative because it bounds the nonnegative
left side; the second follows from exp(-x)>=1-x for x>=0. The suffix
factors of step2 and step1 have modulus at most1 by(28). With
gamma=73/49, (27) and(31) imply

    Gap_h(z)<=2alpha exp(-gamma S alpha)
       <=2/[gamma exp(1) S].                         (32)

The last inequality follows by differentiating x exp(-gamma S x) on
x>=0; its maximum is at x=1/(gamma S). The elementary exponential
series, retaining terms through1/4!, gives exp(1)>65/24. Hence

    2/[gamma exp(1)]<2352/4745<62/125,               (33)

where the last comparison is the exact integer inequality
294000<294190. This is symbolic reasoning, not a numerical evaluation.

In the other regime alpha>=1/100, (28) gives for every prefix step

    |1-rz|^2<=1-r(2-r)alpha
       <=1-r alpha/2<=exp(-r alpha/2).

Thus(27), alpha<=1 and the suffix modulus bounds imply

    Gap_h(z)<=2exp(-S/200)<=160000/S^2.               (34)

For the second inequality use exp(x)>=x^2/2 at x=S/200. If S>=400000,
the final expression is at most2/(5S), strictly below62/(125S).
The zero-prefix-polynomial case and z=0 already have value0. Combining
both regimes proves the full-reference estimate

    sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z)<62/(125S), S>=400000.      (35)

In (35) the strict supremum bound follows from the UNIFORM strict
constant in(33) and the uniform constant2/5 in(34); it does not follow
merely by taking a supremum of pointwise strict inequalities.

Since S>744,

    62/(125S)<1/(2S+12)<=V_lift(S),                  (36)

as seen by multiplying positive denominators to obtain124S+744<125S.
Thus for every mixed word(3) with S>=400000,

    sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)<V_lift(S)
       <K_n(h)=F_ray_n(h)=sup_family V.              (37)

In particular m>=400000 is sufficient, uniformly in u,c. This compares
the entire optimized family and gives a finite actual member realizing
the strict separation from the full complex reference. It does NOT say
that every individual member is better than that reference.

To make that last distinction quantitative, the member b=0 has
V_planar=3/[13(S+1)^2]. The actual real curvature
theta=1/[2(S+5)] lies in D_h because a scalar convex quadratic realizes
its entire transcript. Each factor1-theta h_i is in[0,1], and induction
on the factors gives their product at least1-theta sum h_i=1/2. Hence

    sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)
       >=theta product_i(1-theta h_i)^2
       >=1/[8(S+5)]>V_planar for S>=3.              (38)

The last inequality is equivalent to13S^2+2S-107>0; at S=3 it is16>0
and its derivative26S+2 is positive. This memberwise reversal is why
one must compare the whole family without confusing its supremum with
an arbitrary fixed lift. The complete scalar quadratic channel, and
orthogonal sums of scalar quadratics, are included in the upper bound
through their real parameters and weighted-average normalization.

## 8. ORIGINAL benchmark at the actual horizons

Use exactly the accepted recurrences, with C_0=B_0=1 and

    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.                   (39)

Here C_n=1+S_circle(n), B_n=1+2S_bullet(n) and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. No
asymptotic decimal surrogate is used. Let lambda=1+sqrt(2) and
rho=log_2(lambda)>1. From(39), C_n is nondecreasing: choose j=n-1
to get C_n>=Omega(C_(n-1),1)>C_(n-1). The balanced split also gives

    C_(2^l-1)>=lambda^l, l>=0,

by induction using Omega(a,a)=lambda a. For n>=1 choose the integer l
with2^l<=n<2^(l+1). Then

    B_n>=G(C_(n-1),1)>=2C_(n-1)>=2lambda^l
       >=(2/lambda)n^rho.                            (40)

The final weak inequality follows from n<2^(l+1). On(3),
S<=5m/2 and n=2m+3, so(24),(40) give

    B_(2m+3) V_lift(S)
       >=(2/lambda)(2m+3)^rho/(5m+12) -> infinity.   (41)

The displayed lower bound is independent of u,c, proving uniformity.
In contrast, the accepted bound B_n<=1+4n^p_bd, p_bd=log_2(3)<2,
and S>m give

    B_(2m+3) V_planar
       <=3[1+4(2m+3)^p_bd]/[13(m+1)^2] ->0.         (42)

This is a genuine change in the old retained-triangle diagnostic, but
(26) and(37) show precisely why it contributes no new target strength:
the ray/Huber witness already has the same favorable profile comparison
on these mixed words. An easier family of schedules cannot certify the
all-word infimum. No bound here is an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.

## 9. Exact legal unit-step example and counterexample search

Set the prefix to the single step1, so the full word is(1,2,2,1) and
S=1. Choose a=b=1,H=12. This is a legal nondegenerate member of(6).
Its five actual samples are

    position       gradient      value
    (2p,13)        (p,1)         32
    (p,12)         (p,1)         18
    (-p,10)        (q,1)         14
    (3e,8)         (e,1)         10
    (2e,7)         (e,1)          8.                 (43)

All updates are literal. The unit step from the first sample reaches
its residual(p,12), whose value is18>0, so that residual is not a
minimum. The minimum cone is(16) with a=b=1;0 is the closest initial
minimum and R0^2=52+169=221. The actual payoff is therefore16/221.
The minimum-row products in chronological order are25,11,10,9,7;
all other rows are given explicitly by(13)-(14) at T=1 or0.

The complete ray on this word is1/13=17/221. To check this without
assuming(3), all multiple checkpoints are impossible by the same final
step1 argument in Section6; the singleton values are0,1/28,1/48,0,
and the no-checkpoint value is1/13. Thus(43) is an exact legal
counterexample to the proposed implication

    legal nonminimum-residual passage through a unit step
        implies a strict gain over the complete ray reference.

The implication fails by the positive exact difference1/221. This
does not refute the old theorem whose residual was assumed to be a
minimum; that hypothesis has deliberately been removed.

The stronger search question was whether some finite height, lift
magnitude, moved closest minimum, or negative vertical value component
in this declared family can give V>Huber on a prefix of mass S>=1.
Equation(21) answers it negatively for ALL such legal choices. No
counterexample to that family ceiling survived: the exact identity is
its proof. Attempts to improve the ratio merely by taking a closer
nonzero minimum fail at(17)-(18), and attempts to increase terminal
value without its height/radius payment fail at(20).

This is not evidence against the unrestricted target. The remaining
mechanism would need a genuinely changing added gradient, coupled
transverse geometry, or another redesigned suffix outside(6)-(10).
Their full all-word price is not proved here. Renaming that missing
uniform inequality as a technical lemma would leave a BLOCKED route.

## 10. Research process, scope limits and final allocated handoff

The direct-proving screen was: construct an actual nonminimum state
outside the retained absolute jets; carry every directed row and the
true minimum cost; optimize the ENTIRE changed family analytically;
then compare all declared references on actual mixed physical words.
Sections2-8 supply that full joint argument. The counterexample search
is recorded explicitly in Section9. No isolated unverified lemma is
imported as a correctness premise.

Literature search was conditioned on the live mutation program, namely
a whole-word suffix redesign that raises terminal value relative to
entry-gradient norm. Matlas returned a previously read constant-step
summability lemma (arXiv2310.17610, Lemma3.1) and irrelevant results; it
did not supply an applicable variable-step or all-word theorem. A second
projection/conjugacy query returned arXiv1402.5884 Proposition2.1 and
arXiv2106.11938 Lemma12, neither used as a premise. Primary-source web
retrieval located Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur arXiv1502.05666 again.
The exact local text, Sections2.4-2.5, Theorem4 including its proof and
Corollary1, was read. Its conjugacy/interpolation definitions agree with
the already accepted inputs. The new branch reduces to that audited
projection regime, so no further paper is required to justify its
closure. All needed construction and projection arguments are proved
directly above; no external theorem is imported.

The principal failed inference is now precise: escaping a fixed-jet
obstruction and recovering the inverse-mass rate does NOT imply a new
reference gain. In this entire family the extra common gradient obeys
the exact Huber square identity(20). The explicit lift can beat the FULL
complex reference while remaining strictly weaker than complete K/ray.
The new minimum cone cannot hide a reduction in the true initial radius.

Exact scope exclusions: there is no all-interpolant optimum claim; no
decision of unrestricted W_n on any of these words; no general theorem
about every nonlinear scalar function; no original-H_m result; no
all-word lower-profile proof; and no full-class upper certificate giving
an asymptotic improvement. The original target remains OPEN.

All numerical evaluations, solver calls, machine algebra, replay runs,
formal-verifier calls, installations, consults and native subagents used
in this round: ZERO. File persistence and operational hashes are the
only machine operations beyond textual retrieval. This is one complete
joint proof_attempt, UNVERIFIED, with no new fact_id. This is the final
allocated round; no further round or formal gate is inferred.

# Saturated-cut certificates retain a signed terminal-interpolation credit

Status: self-contained candidate, NOT verifier-accepted. No solver or
fact_submit was used. The full initial-distance objective theorem is not
being restricted here: the statement concerns the explicitly specified
intrinsic primitive energy. The only accepted background used is the
necessity of the interpolation inequalities in fact 1252b8823bb5a290.

## 1. Definitions and an exact identity

Work at smoothness one. On a GD trajectory for a list h of sum s and
capacity C=1+s, write g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i), and

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0,
  V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2.

Take two lists a,b of capacities A,D, respectively, and set

  T=A+D+r,  r>0,  rT=AD,
  kappa=(T/D)^2=(A/r)^2,
  theta=1-1/kappa.

In particular 0<r<min(A,D), kappa>1 and 0<theta<1. The joined
schedule is h=(a,1+r,b). Denote its bridge query by L and the tail
queries by j=0,...,m; z=m is the final query. Tail indices in Q always
mean the corresponding original joined indices. Define

  J_b=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,z),
  E_theta(b)=V_b-theta J_b,
  C_cross=sum_(j<m) b_j Q_(j,L)+Q_(z,L)+r Q_(L,z).

The exact identity, including every value term, is

  V_h = V_a + kappa E_theta(b) - (kappa-1) C_cross.       (1)

All coefficients in -C_cross are nonnegative on actual inequalities
-Q. Thus universal nonnegativity of V_a and E_theta(b) is a sufficient
condition for universal nonnegativity of V_h. It is not necessary here
to assume V_b>=0. Whether this genuinely enlarges the allowable tail
class is a separate question.

Here is a direct audit of (1). Put p=g_L, q=g_z, and
H=sum_(j<m)b_j g_j. Then x_tail,0=x_L-(1+r)p and x_z=x_tail,0-H.
For C=C_cross+J_b the value part is

  C_f=2sum_(j<m)b_j f_j+(r-D)f_L+(2-D-r)f_z.

Expand each Q and use
2sum_j b_j<g_j,sum_(i<j)b_i g_i>=||H||^2-sum_j b_j^2||g_j||^2.
The gradient part reduces exactly to

  C_g=sum_(j<m)b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2
            +[(D-r)/2-r^2]||p||^2+(D+r)||q||^2/2.

All mixed p,H,q products cancel. The identities

  (kappa-1)(D-r)=2(A+r),
  kappa r^2=A^2,  kappa D^2=T^2

then show V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)C coefficient by coefficient:
the f_L coefficient is 2(1+r), the f_z coefficient is -2(T-1),
the p-square coefficient is -r(1+r), and the q-square coefficient
is -T(T-1). Substituting kappa theta=kappa-1 proves (1).

## 2. Full intrinsic certificates force the bridge coefficients

For this part assume the entries of a and b are strictly positive;
either list may be empty. Suppose an explicit intrinsic certificate is
given:

  M_h=V_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij,
  lambda_ij>=0,

where values cancel and M_h is PSD in the full independent formal
gradient vector. Allowing the optimum index and x_0 initially does not
enlarge this class. Indeed V_h has no x_0-square coefficient, so PSD
forces the x_0 row to vanish. Its cross coefficients are -lambda_i,star,
which forces every lambda_i,star=0. Summing the value-flow equalities
then gives sum_i lambda_star,i=0, so these multipliers also vanish.
This argument relies on the absence of an initial-distance term and
does NOT apply to a full objective certificate.

There are two explicit equality trajectories for V_h. A scalar Huber
trajectory staying on one affine ray has every gradient equal to 1,
all finite Q zero, and V_h=0. A second trajectory uses the derivative

  f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)),  eta=r/D,

starting at A. The prefix gradient is 1, its last query is 1, the
bridge lands at -r, and the tail gradient is -eta until its endpoint
-eta. Every incoming optimum residual is zero. Distance telescoping
gives V_h=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0.

For completeness, if R_i is a prefix query's remaining prefix sum
and R_j is a tail query's remaining tail sum, this second trajectory
has

  Q_(prefix i,tail j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j,
  Q_(tail j,prefix i)=-(1+eta)R_i.                       (2)

Every within-block Q is zero. Since M_h>=0 and all lambda Q<=0,
equality of V_h forces multiplier complementarity and M_h to vanish
on each equality gradient vector. Those vectors span the two block
indicators e_A,e_B, so both indicators lie in the kernel of M_h.

By (2), the only potentially nonzero cross multipliers are

  nu_i=lambda_(prefix i,z),  mu_j=lambda_(tail j,L).

For a prefix index i<L, the corresponding row of M_h e_B is
-D nu_i/2. Thus nu_i=0 for every i<L. Write nu=nu_L.
For a nonterminal tail index j, the corresponding row of M_h e_A
is [r mu_j-nu b_j]/2; at z it is [r mu_z-nu]/2.
Consequently

  mu_j=(nu/r)b_j  (j<m),  mu_z=nu/r.

The sum of the prefix value-flow equations is
sum_j mu_j-nu=2(A+r). Therefore all cross multipliers are fixed:

  lambda_(j,L)=(kappa-1)b_j (j<m),
  lambda_(z,L)=kappa-1,
  lambda_(L,z)=r(kappa-1),                              (3)

and every other cross multiplier is zero. The mixed gradient block
of the full matrix vanishes, not merely its value on equality data.

## 3. Exact cone interface and the sign that cannot be discarded

Let lambda^A,lambda^B be the two within-block multiplier arrays of
the given full certificate. Define a signed tail array by

  lambda_hat_ij=lambda^B_ij/kappa
                 -theta b_i * 1_(i<m,j=z).             (4)

Combining (1) with (3) yields the full block-diagonal identity

  M_h = M_a + kappa M_b,
  M_a=V_a+sum lambda^A_ij Q_ij,
  M_b=V_b+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij.                       (5)

Each summand has its own value cancellation, since its values are in
a disjoint block. Full PSD is equivalent to PSD of both summands.
The prefix multipliers are nonnegative. The exact tail constraints are

  lambda_hat_ij>=0 except possibly i<m,j=z,
  lambda_hat_(i,z)>=-theta b_i.                        (6)

Conversely, any prefix intrinsic certificate and any PSD tail identity
with the value cancellation and bounds (6) give a nonnegative full
certificate by (3),(4). Equivalently, the tail has an ordinary
nonnegative certificate for E_theta(b), not necessarily for V_b.

Thus direct restriction to an ordinary primitive tail certificate would
require the stronger bounds

  lambda^B_(i,z)>=(kappa-1)b_i.                        (7)

They are not implied for each full certificate. The next exact example
proves this, including full nonnegativity and PSD.

## 4. Exact full certificate violating the proposed coefficient floor

Use the empty prefix A=1, tail b=(1,1) of capacity D=3, and

  r=sqrt(7)-2, T=sqrt(7)+2, kappa=(11+4sqrt(7))/9.

The full schedule is h=(sqrt(7)-1,1,1). Use original indices 0,1,2,3,
where 0 is the bridge query and 1,2,3 are the tail queries. Set

  lambda_10=lambda_20=lambda_30=kappa-1,
  lambda_03=r(kappa-1),
  lambda_12=5kappa/2, lambda_21=17kappa/4,
  lambda_23=37kappa/4-1, lambda_32=12kappa,
  lambda_13=3kappa/4-1,

with every unlisted multiplier zero. All are nonnegative, since
kappa>4/3. Direct substitution, or (1) and the audited tail identity
below, gives

  V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
   =kappa { ||(g_1-g_2)-(g_2-g_3)/8||^2
                         +(255/64)||g_2-g_3||^2 } >=0. (8)

For the tail alone, write u=g_0-g_1 and v=g_1-g_2 using local indices.
Its signed multiplier list is

  lambda_hat_01=5/2, lambda_hat_10=17/4,
  lambda_hat_12=33/4, lambda_hat_21=12,
  lambda_hat_02=-1/4, lambda_hat_20=0.

The value coefficients cancel, and expansion gives

  V_(1,1)+sum lambda_hat_ij Q_ij
      =||u||^2-<u,v>/4+4||v||^2
      =||u-v/8||^2+(255/64)||v||^2.

Nevertheless lambda_13=(kappa-1)-kappa/4 is strictly below the
proposed floor kappa-1. The restricted terminal coefficient is -1/4.
This refutes the inference of (7) from full nonnegativity and PSD.

There IS another ordinary primitive certificate for this tail:
lambda_01=2,lambda_10=4,lambda_12=8,lambda_21=12, all other
multipliers zero, gives ||u||^2+4||v||^2. Accordingly this example
does not disprove existence of a suitable re-selection, arbitrary-tail
heredity, or the original asymptotic minimax problem. It identifies
the exact missing sign assertion rather than replacing it with PSD.

## 5. A particular nonprimitive tail cannot exploit this credit

The previously considered b=(17/10,2,6/5) fails even E_theta for
every 0<=theta<=1. Here is a self-contained witness.
Put m=7/34, ell=21/85. Define the continuous derivative

  f'(x)=x                  for x<=m,
         m                 for m<=x<=m+ell,
         x-ell             for x>=m+ell,

and integrate with f(0)=0. Its derivative is nondecreasing and
1-Lipschitz, so f is convex differentiable 1-smooth with minimizer 0.
Starting at x_0=106/85 gives

  (x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(106/85,-77/170,77/170,7/34),
  (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(1,-77/170,7/34,7/34).

The endpoint is core: x_3=g_3=m, f_3=m^2/2. Define
I_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Direct scalar evaluation gives

  I_0=ell(1-m)=567/2890, I_1=I_2=I_3=0,
  sum_(i<3)b_i I_i=567/1700,
  V_b=-13581/23120.

At a core endpoint, expansion gives Q_(i,3)=-I_i. Thus

  E_theta(b)=-13581/23120+theta*567/1700
           <= -29349/115600 <0.                       (9)

The value of V_b can also be checked without integrating every term:
distance telescoping gives
V_b=x_0^2-D^2g_3^2-2sum b_i I_i, with D=59/10.
This proves (9) exactly.

Consequently this specific tail cannot acquire a valid E_theta
certificate at any saturated join parameter. A rank-one coefficient
matching attempt on this same fixed tail also failed, but (9) is the
stronger actual-function obstruction. Neither statement excludes other
nonprimitive tails. No numerical schedule screen was reopened.

# Asymmetric centered-simplex folding: positive heights and an inverse-width cost

**Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED RESEARCH DRAFT.** Worker max2, single allocated
invocation after ended baseline67, ceiling68/attempted69. No verifier call
or fact_submit is authorized or performed. This is neither a fact nor a
resolution of the original minimax problem.

One complete multilevel construction law is analyzed, together with ALL
smooth convex completions of its complete queried prefix. A separate
three-coordinate, one-step scope test is included; it supplies no full-word
performance assertion. This stays within the allocation of at most two laws.

The complete law has arbitrarily many distinct levels, an arbitrarily large
finite dimension, no extra carrier direction, nonacute simplex vertices,
strictly positive queried heights at EVERY physical query, and coordinates
which repeatedly enter and leave the active face. Its initial projected
coordinates have at least THREE distinct levels. Nevertheless its whole
parameter domain has, on every SAME-enclosing-m H_(m,1/4), m>=1536,

    E(K,h) < 9 / T_h,
    T_h := product_(t<n) h_t/(h_t-1) > 3^(2^(m-1)),                 (A)

for EVERY legal completion K of the complete old prefix and the optimum
jet, normalized by K's OWN closest initial distance. In particular this
includes the original centered-simplex objective and its pointwise maximal
prefix completion. Thus this law does not improve the COMPLETE K/ray
channel; it lies strictly below even the sum-based Huber member. The
original all-split benchmark-scaled upper bound tends to zero.

The obstruction is the special Cartesian construction: BOTH inverse
branches are populated over EVERY future coordinate level. Its exact
extreme-coordinate cost is exponential in the number of physical leaves.
This is not a universal simplex obstruction. Section 10 gives an actual
three-level centered-simplex state on the original first leaf for which
the purported universal extreme-coordinate inequality is false.

All new arguments are assembled jointly below. No unverified earlier
claim, independent-gradient model, expected face, or common-mode model
is imported as a premise.

## 1. Original target, accepted inputs, and exact reference scopes

For every finite nonnegative schedule h of length n and every L>0 the
original problem asks about the supremum W_n(h) of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2),
    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L) grad f(x_t),

over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions with
L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum, and every x_0!=x_star.
The required original affirmative conclusion remains the schedule-uniform
all-horizon inequality W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n. W=2R throughout. A diagnostic
on one word family, even a useful one, would not prove that conclusion.

The original all-INTEGER-split benchmark is retained literally:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    Gbench(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gbench(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.                                               (1)

Every maximum is over all integer splits, not a balanced or restricted
tree. No recurrence was executed.

Accepted inputs, read at their exact scopes:

* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290: all ordered smooth-convex interpolation
  inequalities, and the pointwise maximal full-prefix conjugate extension.
* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d: the complete single-cut channel

      K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),
        max_(j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/((1+P_j)^2(1+2T_j))},
      S=sum_t h_t, P_j=sum_(t<j)h_t, T_j=sum_(t>j)h_t.

* fact_id1fca3e5dd555cb4a: the COMPLETE alternating-ray channel F_ray_n,
  with all feasible original chronological checkpoint subsets, all its
  retained offsets and areas, and its terminal optimization, satisfies
  K_n<=F_ray_n. Only this containment is used here.
* fact_id5fd396eba75651e7, equation (77) and its stated m,tau domain:

      B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m) < 237/250,
      h=H_(m,1/4), m>=1536.                                    (2)

Its spiral-family obstructions are not asserted for the new simplex law.
The earlier unverified findings8bf359c98a737933 and aa3c5e1d91baf405 were
used only for awareness of scalar, cone, and all-history failure scopes.

For the diagnostic set, with the SAME enclosing m in EVERY child,

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, S=S_m.                          (3)

All actual physical entries, repeated queries, and the unqueried terminal
leaf are retained. Here h_t>1. In particular the construction below does
NOT claim to handle all arbitrary nonnegative schedules: its inverse
formulas require h_t>1. The original goal and its zero-step quantifier
are unchanged and remain open.

Counting the occurrences of each tree level gives

    S_j+1=(1-v)2^j+v c^j, v=beta/(c(c-2)), 0<v<1,
    S+1<c^m, 1+2S<2c^m, 2<c<5/2, 1/3<a<1/2.                  (4)

Indeed the total is 2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2); c>lambda and
lambda(lambda-2)=1 imply v<1. For the crude bounds, beta>999/1000,
lambda<121/50, and m>=1536 give c<5/2 and a>1/3. Also
a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<1/2. All of these are symbolic inequalities.

## 2. The real centered simplex and its static smooth objective

Let N>=3 be an integer, let E_N={x in R^N:sum_j x_j=0} with the
inherited Euclidean metric, let u=(1/N,...,1/N), and choose sigma>0.
The intrinsic real dimension is N-1. Define every slope and every offset by

    v_j=sigma(e_j-u), 1<=j<=N,       offset_j=0,
    D=conv{v_1,...,v_N},
    J(y)=max_j <v_j,y>,
    F(x)=max_(g in D)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2), x in E_N.                (5)

There is no additional coordinate, positive carrier, variable metric, or
rescaling of an iterate. The Gram of the REAL slopes is

    <v_i,v_j>=sigma^2(1_(i=j)-1/N).                            (6)

For a literal R^(N-1) realization, an orthonormal basis of E_N is

    b_k=(e_1+...+e_k-k e_(k+1))/sqrt(k(k+1)), 1<=k<N.

The matrix U with these columns satisfies U^T U=I and U U^T=I-11^T/N.
The exact isometry X -> U X turns the slopes into sigma U^T e_j,
and leaves the scale sigma and all inner products unchanged. No Walsh
representation is needed or invoked.

The maximizer g=P_D(x) is unique by strict convexity of squared distance.
It is characterized by

    g in D, <x-g,v_j-g><=0 for ALL j.                           (7)

Adding the corresponding inequalities at x and x' gives
<x-x',g-g'>>=||g-g'||^2, hence P_D is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating (5)
at the two maximizers bounds the differentiability remainder between 0
and ||x-x'||^2; thus F is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable,
and grad F=P_D. Its least gradient Lipschitz constant is exactly1:
0 is interior to D in E_N, and the gradient equals x near0.

Its COMPLETE minimum set and value are

    argmin F={0}, min F=0.                                    (8)

To see uniqueness, for nonzero x in E_N some x_j>0. Then
<x,v_j>=sigma x_j>0 and a sufficiently small positive multiple of
v_j belongs to D and makes the expression (5) positive. The value at0
is zero. Thus the exact initial closest radius is always ||x_0||.

## 3. Exact threshold and every ordered row

Write x=(sigma/N)w with sum_j w_j=0, and let theta be the unique real
number satisfying

    (1/N)sum_j (1+w_j-theta)_+ =1.                             (9)

It is nonnegative: at theta0 the sum is at least sum_j(1+w_j)=N.
Set P_j=(1+w_j-theta)_+. Then

    g_j=(sigma/N)(P_j-1),
    y_j=(x-g)_j=(sigma/N)min(1+w_j,theta),
    delta=<y,g>=sigma^2 theta/N,
    2F(x)=(sigma^2/N)(2theta+(1/N)sum_j(P_j-1)^2).              (10)

These are exact finite-coordinate expressions, not expectations. The
probability-simplex coordinates of g/sigma+u are P_j/N.

For completeness, sum P_j=N implies sum min(1+w_j,theta)=0.
On every coordinate with P_j>0 the residual equals sigma theta/N;
on the others it is no larger. Consequently <y,v_j><=sigma^2 theta/N
for every j, with equality on the active support. This proves (7)
against every vertex, including all remote ones, and proves (10).
It also proves delta=0 iff x lies in D; a first zero height by itself
says nothing about later heights.

For ANY two actual samples, terminal and optimum included, put
g_i=P_D(x_i), y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=F(x_i). Direct expansion yields

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                   (11)

Thus ALL ordered remote, opposite, optimum and terminal rows hold on
ONE fixed function. Every Gram is the actual real Euclidean Gram.
The same argument covers repeated points, without deleting any query.

Under a physical step h=1+d>1 the exact coordinate map is

    w'_j=w_j+h-h(1+w_j-theta)_+,
         =w_j+h                    on an inactive coordinate,
         =-d w_j+h theta           on an active coordinate.    (12)

In particular active coordinates reverse their relative order. No
order-preservation assumption will be made.

## 4. A fixed finite Cartesian inverse-folding law

The following domain is the ENTIRE domain of the construction analyzed
here. Choose any finite word with h_t>1, set d_t=h_t-1, and choose a
terminal list z_1,...,z_b of real numbers, b>=2, with mean0 and at
least two distinct entries. The list, b, and sigma may depend on the
whole known word and, on the diagnostic, on m.

For each t choose a rational number p_t in (0,1), write
p_t=A_t/D_t with integers 1<=A_t<D_t, and let q_t=1-p_t. Do not
interpret p_t as an approximate face mass. It is the EXACT count
fraction of a Cartesian address set. Use coordinates labeled by

    omega=(k,j_0,...,j_(n-1)),
    1<=k<=b, 1<=j_t<=D_t,
    N=b product_(t<n) D_t.                                   (13)

Define the fixed branch label H_t by j_t<=A_t and L_t otherwise.
(Branch labels H_t,L_t in this section are not the physical words H_j
or the endpoint magnitudes below.) Define all coordinate levels BEFORE
the forward run by

    w_n(omega)=z_k,
    theta_t=d_t q_t/p_t,
    w_t(omega)=w_(t+1)(omega)-h_t             if label L_t,
    w_t(omega)=h_t q_t/p_t-w_(t+1)(omega)/d_t if label H_t.      (14)

These are finite mathematical definitions, not executed trajectories.
Equivalently put alpha_t=1, b_t=-h_t on L_t, and alpha_t=-1/d_t,
b_t=h_t q_t/p_t on H_t. Then the explicit initial level is

    w_0(omega)=z_k product_(t<n)alpha_t
        +sum_(t<n)b_t product_(r<t)alpha_r.                    (15)

Choose precisely the following guard, and no hidden cone or radius guard:

    max_omega w_(t+1)(omega) < d_t/p_t, EVERY 0<=t<n.           (16)

The domain is nonempty for every terminal list and every word h_t>1.
Starting backward, if R=max w_(t+1)>0, the purely mathematical choice

    p_t=1/[2(ceil(R/d_t)+1)]                                  (17)

is rational, lies in (0,1), and gives the strict guard. This fixes all
parameters before the actual forward run. No p_t, face, or threshold
is changed after observing that run. Every other rational array
satisfying (16) is allowed in the theorem, however uneven or m-dependent.

The actual initial state is x_0=(sigma/N)w_0. The objective is exactly
(5) with the full N-vertex simplex, not a projected or normalized path.

## 5. Full evolution, multilevel gradients, and genuine face returns

All averages below are literal arithmetic means over the full coordinate
set (13). Because w_(t+1) ignores address j_t, every future level occurs
with EXACTLY the fraction p_t of H_t addresses and q_t of L_t addresses.
Thus, if the mean of w_(t+1) is zero, (14) gives mean

    q_t(-h_t)+p_t h_t q_t/p_t=0.

Backward induction gives sum w_t=0 for every t. On the H_t addresses,

    1+w_t-theta_t=1/p_t-w_(t+1)/d_t >0,

and on L_t addresses,

    1+w_t-theta_t=w_(t+1)-d_t/p_t <0.                         (18)

The average positive part is p_t*(1/p_t)=1 because the future mean
is zero. Hence (9) identifies the ACTUAL threshold as theta_t. Substituting
into (12) gives exactly w_(t+1) on BOTH branches. Induction through EVERY
original index proves that (14) is the whole actual forward trajectory.
This proves, rather than prescribes, the active faces.

Every queried height is strictly positive:

    delta_t=(sigma^2/N)d_t q_t/p_t >0, 0<=t<n.                (19)

There are at least two different future coordinate values at each t:
the original terminal list is nonconstant, and either branch in (14)
is injective on future values. At time t the H_t positive projection
values therefore include at least two distinct numbers, while the
L_t coordinates have projection value0. The INITIAL PROJECTION has
at least THREE distinct coordinate levels; in fact this holds at
every queried time. The initial input levels have at least four
distinct values: each branch has at least two, and the strict threshold
separates the two branches. Thus this is not a two-group barycentric line.

For n>=3 each address pattern H_t,L_(t+1),H_(t+2) exists, as do all
other patterns. Its coordinate leaves and then re-enters the active
face, always under positive queried heights. Entire earlier active
subsets can split into active and inactive parts at the next step.
These are actual cap returns on the one objective; no fixed active face,
permanent core, face expectation, or sign absorption is assumed.

## 6. Exact true radius, terminal formula, and the width price

To avoid confusing endpoint magnitudes with the smoothness L, put

    ell_t=-min_omega w_t(omega), r_t=max_omega w_t(omega),
    Wd_t=ell_t+r_t, V_t=(1/N)sum_omega w_t(omega)^2.

Every nonconstant mean-zero state has ell_t,r_t>0. All are finite.
Since the same full future list occurs on both branches, (18) gives

    ell_t=ell_(t+1)+h_t,
    r_t=h_t q_t/p_t+ell_(t+1)/d_t,
    Wd_t=h_t(1/p_t+ell_(t+1)/d_t),
    Wd_t >= (h_t/d_t) Wd_(t+1).                              (20)

Here the LAST inequality uses the guard r_(t+1)<d_t/p_t. To check
the extremes without an ordering assumption, the lowest L_t value is
-ell_(t+1)-h_t. The lowest H_t value is greater than d_t/p_t-h_t,
so the former is the minimum. The greatest H_t value is
h_t q_t/p_t+ell_(t+1)/d_t; it exceeds every L_t value since the
greatest L_t value is less than d_t/p_t-h_t, and their difference
is greater than 1/p_t+ell_(t+1)/d_t. This proves (20).

The exact variance and gradient-norm formulas are

    V_t=(q_t+p_t/d_t^2)V_(t+1)+h_t^2 q_t/p_t,
    G_t:=(N/sigma^2)||g_t||^2=q_t/p_t+p_t V_(t+1)/d_t^2,
    dist(x_0,argmin F)^2=||x_0||^2=(sigma^2/N)V_0.             (21)

Cross terms vanish by the exact mean-zero identity, not by randomness.
An explicit formula for V_0 is

    V_0=V_n product_(t<n) Avar_t
           +sum_(t<n) Bvar_t product_(r<t) Avar_r,
    Avar_t=q_t+p_t/d_t^2, Bvar_t=h_t^2 q_t/p_t.                (22)

At the ACTUAL terminal, let theta_n be the unique solution of

    (1/b)sum_(k<=b)(1+z_k-theta_n)_+=1,
    G_n=(1/b)sum_(k<=b)[(1+z_k-theta_n)_+-1]^2.

Then the exact attained terminal formula on the original static function is

    E(F,h)=(2theta_n+G_n)/V_0.                                (23)

No assumption places this terminal in the quadratic core. Because
F(x)<=max_j<x,v_j>=sigma max_j x_j, one also has

    2theta_n+G_n <= 2r_n.                                    (24)

Thus the formula covers arbitrary terminal offsets, negative tails,
terminal levels, and terminal active sets in the whole declared domain.

For later estimates define the positive finite products

    T_h=product_(t<n)h_t/d_t,
    T_pre=product_(t<n-1)h_t/d_t.

The exact first branching cost can be written

    Bvar_0=h_0 Wd_0-(h_0^2/d_0)(ell_n+S-1).                   (25)

This follows from Wd_0=h_0(1/p_0+ell_1/d_0),
ell_0=ell_n+S, and ell_1+d_0=ell_0-1. In particular it remains
valid for arbitrarily large terminal ell_n. There is no bounded-radius
or bounded-terminal-level assumption.

## 7. Every full-prefix completion and its OWN radius

Let C_pre be the class of ALL finite-dimensional convex differentiable
1-smooth functions K which preserve EVERY queried triple
(x_t,g_t,f_t), 0<=t<n, from the construction and the optimum triple
(0,0,0). Extra Euclidean coordinates are allowed by isometric embedding
of the original vectors. Each K has an attained minimum0. By induction
its GD run reaches the same x_n; its terminal gradient is its OWN
gradient, with no prescribed value.

The class is nonempty because F belongs to it. Accepted1252 gives its
pointwise maximal member on E_N as

    H_pre(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,max_(t<n)(<y_t,v>-delta_t)),
    F_pre(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H_pre(v)).                            (26)

All coefficients are fixed by the full old word and old function before
F_pre's own run. As every delta_t>0, the active affine function at0 is
uniquely the zero function; therefore partial H_pre(0)={0}. Equivalently
F_pre(x)=0 iff x=0, since Fenchel equality at v0 is equivalent to
x in partial H_pre(0). Thus its COMPLETE minimum is {0}, its value
is0, and its true initial radius is also sqrt((sigma^2/N)V_0).
It need not be a simplex projection at unqueried points. All ordered
interpolation rows hold for it by accepted1252, including its actual
terminal. A change of function is never made DURING either run.

For an arbitrary K in C_pre, complete the terminal-gradient square in
its ORIGINAL outgoing row Q_(n,n-1)<=0. Because the actual last step
is h_(n-1)=1+d_(n-1), this gives

    2K(x_n) <= 2delta_(n-1)+d_(n-1)^2 ||g_(n-1)||^2
              =(sigma^2/N) U_last,
    U_last=(d^2+2d)q/p+p V_n,                                (27)

where d,p,q refer only to the last index. This does not assume an
old terminal gradient or an old terminal height.

Every number z_k lies in [-ell_n,r_n]. Averaging
(z_k+ell_n)(r_n-z_k)>=0 yields V_n<=ell_n r_n. The last guard
gives p r_n<d. Using h=1+d at that last index, (27) implies

    U_last <= h^2/p+d ell_n
             <= h^2(1/p+ell_n/d)=h Wd_(n-1).                 (28)

Paying each completion's own radius is essential. For ANY of its own
minimizers z, its original initial optimum row gives

    <g_0,x_0-z> >= f_0+||g_0||^2/2=delta_0+||g_0||^2.

Taking the closest such minimizer and using Cauchy--Schwarz proves

    rho_K^2:=dist(x_0,argmin K)^2
       >= (delta_0+||g_0||^2)^2/||g_0||^2
       >= ||g_0||^2+2delta_0
       >= (sigma^2/N)(1+2d_0)q_0/p_0.                       (29)

In particular rho_K is strictly positive. When 0<d_0<1/2,

    (1+2d_0)/h_0^2=1-(d_0/h_0)^2>8/9,
    rho_K^2 > (8/9)(sigma^2/N) Bvar_0.                       (30)

Thus the whole completion class has a paid radius bound, even when
its complete minimum set differs from that of F or F_pre. Pointwise
maximality is NOT used to transfer a radius.

## 8. The full-word quantitative failure on the entire H_(m,1/4)

Return to (3) with EVERY integer m>=1536. Write d=a for the common
first and last excess and h_leaf=1+a. The inequalities in (4) give

    1/3<a<1/2, h_leaf<3/2, h_leaf/a<4.

There are precisely 2^(m-1) physical leaves, each with ratio
h_leaf/a=1+1/a>3; every other h_t/d_t is greater than1. Hence

    T_h>3^(2^(m-1)), T_pre=T_h a/h_leaf.                      (31)

For m>=4 the elementary induction 2^(m-1)>=m+4 holds (equality
at m4 and preservation on doubling). Together with S<c^m<3^m it
implies T_h>81*3^m>64S. In particular these estimates hold on the
entire declared m>=1536 domain, without any replay of a word.

From (20), Wd_0>=T_pre Wd_(n-1), while

    Wd_(n-1)=h_leaf(1/p+ell_n/a)
       >=h_leaf,  >=h_leaf ell_n/a.

Consequently

    ell_n+S-1 <= ell_n+S
       <=[(a+S)/h_leaf] Wd_(n-1).

Insert this in (25), using h_0=h_leaf and d_0=a:

    Bvar_0 >= h_leaf[T_pre-(a+S)/a] Wd_(n-1).                (32)

Now T_h>64S implies
T_pre=T_h a/h_leaf>(128/9)S>8S, whereas
2(a+S)/a<2+6S<=8S for S>=1. Therefore

    Bvar_0 > (h_leaf/2) T_pre Wd_(n-1).                      (33)

This estimate retains ARBITRARILY large ell_n and every rational
parameter array satisfying the original guards. It did not replace
the domain by a symmetric start or a small cone.

Combining (27)--(30) and (33), for EVERY K in C_pre, gives

    E(K,h):=2K(x_n)/rho_K^2
       < (9/8) U_last/Bvar_0
       <= 9/(4 T_pre)
       =9 h_leaf/(4a T_h)<9/T_h.                            (34)

This includes F, F_pre, and all completions with additional dimensions
and their own possibly closer minimizers. It also covers every terminal
level array in the stated multigroup domain. No assertion of all-zero
queried heights was made: (19) is strictly positive at EVERY query.

Because T_h>64S>9(1+2S) for S>=1, and using the accepted complete
reference containments, the explicit entire-word comparisons are

    E(K,H_m) < H_base(H_m)=1/(1+2S)
                 <=K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m),                  (35)
    E(K,H_m)/H_base(H_m)
        <18 c^m 3^(-2^(m-1)),
    B_n E(K,H_m)
        <(237/250)*18 c^m 3^(-2^(m-1)) ->0.                 (36)

For the limit, c<5/2 uniformly, while 2^(m-1)-m tends to infinity.
Thus the bounds are uniform over ALL parameters and dimensions in
this construction family. These are upper bounds only for the named
witness/completion family, not for unrestricted W_n(H_m).

Multiplying F or any K by any L>0 and using the original h_t/L
leaves every iterate unchanged and gives exactly the same payoff with
the factor L in its denominator. Thus the normalization remains W=2R.

## 9. Direct proof attempts and why the construction does not give a gain

The first attempt was to use all binary face choices, fixed in advance,
to produce real repeated cap returns without a carrier or an assumed
monotone order. Equations (14)--(19) meet that geometric requirement
on the full original physical word, with at least three projection levels.
The terminal expression (23), however, must pay the initial V_0.
The endpoint identity (20) forces a factor h_t/(h_t-1) at EVERY
inverse fold. The many original short leaves therefore impose the
exponential width price (31). The first branch already converts that
price into the radius charge (25), not merely into a rare uncharged
extreme coordinate. This kills the whole allowed parameter domain.

The second attempt kept ALL those positive-height prefix jets and freed
the terminal by using the maximal smooth-convex completion (26).
The exact original last outgoing row bounds its possible gain by (27).
More strongly, (29)--(34) pay the radius separately for every completion,
so allowing another minimizer or additional ambient coordinates cannot
repair this particular prefix. This is a full-prefix completion failure,
not a restatement of scalar symmetry or zero-height annihilation.

No extra law is asserted from merely solving the finite threshold
recurrence. The first requested attained improvement over the whole
K/ray channel was NOT obtained. The original schedule-uniform
all-split B_n comparison remains completely unpaid.

## 10. Counterexample to extending the width claim to general multilevel starts

This is a scope test, not a favorable full-word witness claim. It uses
the SAME centered-simplex objective (5), now N=3, arbitrary sigma>0,
and the three distinct initial levels

    w_0=(3,1,-4), x_0=(sigma/3)w_0.

The full minimum is {0}, initial radius squared is 26sigma^2/9,
all slopes/offsets/metric are exactly (5), and every ordered row is
covered by (11). The exact initial threshold and gradient coordinates are

    theta_0=3/2,
    (1+w_0-theta_0)_+=(5/2,1/2,0),
    g_0=(sigma/3)(3/2,-1/2,-1),
    delta_0=sigma^2/2>0.                                    (37)

Thus both the input and the actual projection have THREE distinct
levels. Apply the ORIGINAL first physical leaf h_0=1+a of H_(m,1/4):

    w_1=(3/2-3a/2, 3/2+a/2, -3+a).

For 1/3<a<1/2 the second coordinate is largest and the third is
smallest. Hence Wd_0=7 and Wd_1=(9-a)/2. But

    [(1+a)/a] Wd_1-Wd_0=(9-6a-a^2)/(2a)>0.                 (38)

This is the STRICT REVERSE of Wd_0>=(h_0/d_0)Wd_1. Therefore
(20) cannot be extended to arbitrary asymmetric centered-simplex starts,
even on the ACTUAL first leaf with positive height and three projected
levels. The Cartesian duplication of BOTH inverse branches above EVERY
future level is the essential hypothesis in the failure proof.

The static N3 function can of course be run on the entire same word
using (9)--(12), but no useful terminal estimate for that run is proved
here. Neither the first-step formula nor the general finite recurrence
is offered as a gain, a family closure, or a solution of the assignment's
quantitative target.

## 11. Remaining correlated interface, with no new law claimed

For awareness only, the exact algebra explaining what our scope leaves
open is the following. Fix any centered future list w' with mean0 and
an arbitrary proper nonempty label set A of fraction p. Let
mu=(1/N)sum_(j in A)w'_j and d=h-1>0. The inverse candidates are

    w_j=w'_j-h                         outside A,
    w_j=(h theta-w'_j)/d               inside A,
    theta=[mu+d(1-p)]/p.                                   (39)

If theta>0 and max_j w'_j<theta+d, then each designated high branch
is strictly active and each low branch strictly inactive, the mean is
zero, and the average positive part is1. Thus (39) follows directly
by summing (12). In the Cartesian law mu=0 and both extrema occur
in both branches; in a correlated assignment neither property need hold.

Equation (39) is a structural interface, NOT a second completed law or
a verified lemma to build on. It supplies no controlled sequence of
sets, no parameter-uniform initial radius, and no full-word terminal
gain. Both the required all-history selection inequality and the final
all-schedule exact B_n comparison remain open. No automatic further
invocation, formal allowance, or computational allowance is inferred.

## 12. Retrieval, resource ledger, and scope of closeout

The query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results and
construct-counterexamples skills informed recall and the two proof
attempts. The active search program was a mature mutation from
two-level/guarded-carrier starts to genuine multilevel threshold folds;
the missing mechanism was useful full-word positive-height memory
after paying the true radius.

Matlas search_arxiv_theorems was called once for simplex threshold and
overrelaxed projection dynamics. It returned1810.11640 Definition3,
0707.0871 Lemma1 and2211.05205 Corollary5.1; none supplies the needed
terminal theorem and none is imported as a proof dependency. Web search
located the already locally downloaded exact paper:

    Weiran Wang and Miguel A. Carreira-Perpinan,
    Projection onto the probability simplex: An efficient algorithm
    with a simple proof, and an application, arXiv:1309.1541 (2013),
    Section3, Theorem1 and its KKT proof.
    https://arxiv.org/abs/1309.1541

Its extracted proof was read from downloads/1309.1541_public_pdf_text.txt.
It concerns the ordinary Euclidean projection onto nonnegative vectors
of sum1. Our exact change of variables is z=(1+w)/N, threshold theta/N,
and gradient sigma(P/N-u); Section3 rederives the needed projection
and normal facts explicitly. No literature result is an unproved black
box or an external premise in the new joint draft.
